APM PMS Q&A SOP: the session run on Wednesday 4-Nov, on the 3-Nov close

Readings as at Tue 3-Nov-2026, Tuesday's close (the dashboard data built at 06:13 UK on 4-Nov). The run started at 11:11 UK on Wednesday, before Wednesday's close, so Tuesday's close is the latest. The previous note read Mon 2-Nov; this one adds one session, so most readings have moved little and the sections say so. The rules are your Position Entry and Exit Rules as changed on 2-Nov-2026: the 10-day stop-loss rule for Early Innings Probing Bets, the six Sell Criteria alone for Middle Innings Core Investments. The next reading is Fri 6-Nov (Industry RS below 30; the relative 200D MA flat or falling over the month); the next month-end reading is Mon 30-Nov. The next Tuesday session is 10-Nov.

3. Your 3-Nov rule, "We cannot own stocks that are not upgrading over N3-6M - its an inviolate rule", is not in the Position Entry and Exit Rules, but on the record of the last three and six months seven of the 16 holdings would fail it, and four more pass only on upgrades that have stopped or are turning. Failing: Xvivo, Theon, Ballys Intralot, CVS Group, D'Ieteren, Fevertree and Carlsberg (FY2027 EPS down over three and six months; the last month's rises are currency). Passing: Hexatronic, Volex and Sdiptech. The rule looks forward ("N3-6M"), so the past three to six months are evidence, not the test. Each section puts it to you as a For your ruling.

How this note is built

Group 1 (Market) sets the week, the fortnight and the month and answers your 3-Nov market note. Group 2 (Individual Positions) takes the 16 holdings in the order they most need your thinking. Group 3 (Portfolio) gives the themes across holdings, each holding's add stage, the graded add and exit candidates and the proposed order of sale under the link; then what I will do unless you say otherwise, and the data faults.

The same rebuild lost your keep records for Alfen and Hilton Food, mis-dated a Helios Towers tranche and dropped two of Xvivo's three tranches; the rule outcomes here follow the morning readings, which carry your 2-Nov rulings (see the data faults).

Group 1 (Market)

The Stoxx Europe 600 fell 0.6% on Tuesday 3-Nov-2026, which leaves it up only 0.3% in the week, 1.1% over the fortnight and 1.9% over the month; over three months it is down 1.9%, over twelve months up 12.8%. The bases: the week is the 5 sessions from the 27-Oct close, the fortnight the 10 sessions from the 20-Oct close and the month the 21 sessions from the 5-Oct close, the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's bases; the industry figures are price moves of each industry's index over the same sessions (the Market chart, 3 and 12 months).

  • The month's leaders (21 sessions to 3-Nov): Consumer staples +9.3%, Healthcare +8.4%, Professional, business and consumer services +6.6%, Consumer discretionary +5.3%, Infrastructure +3.9%, Defence +3.7% and Real assets +3.7%.
  • The month's laggards: Technology (2.8%), Energy, commodities and metals mining (2.0%), Industrials and capital goods (1.2%), Financials +0.4% and Materials +0.5%.
  • The week: Consumer discretionary +1.9% and Defence +1.6% led, Consumer staples and Healthcare +1.0% each; Energy, commodities and metals mining (2.8%), Technology (2.6%) and Transportation (0.7%) fell. Industrials and capital goods rose 0.3%.
  • Tuesday 3-Nov: Energy, commodities and metals mining (1.0%), Technology (0.9%), Consumer discretionary (0.9%) and Consumer staples (0.5%) led the fall; Telecoms +0.2%, Healthcare +0.1% and Financials +0.1% held.
  • Over three months the split is wider: Transportation +8.7%, Healthcare +7.6% and Energy, commodities and metals mining +4.0%; Real assets (7.6%), Defence (7.1%) and Media (6.2%).

The month's defensive leadership paused this week: Consumer staples, which led the month with +9.3%, rose only 1.0% in the week and fell 0.5% on Tuesday, while Healthcare kept rising. On 3-Nov Fevertree fell 2.4% with Diageo, Remy Cointreau and Coca-Cola Europacific Partners, and Carlsberg eased 0.5% from its 52W high; Hilton Food fell 1.5% with the UK food makers. Against the day, CVS Group closed up at 1,367p while the index fell 0.6%, and Hexatronic rose 1.4% while Technology fell 0.9%. What would confirm a pause rather than a turn: Consumer staples holding its month's gain through the 6-Nov Friday reading; a second week of Consumer staples falling while Healthcare rises would say the rotation is narrowing to Healthcare.

Industrials and capital goods, the book's largest industry (about 21.9% of capital on current values), is the month's third-weakest, and its Industry RS reads 46 on 3-Nov, after 79 on 2-Oct. Below 30 on Fri 6-Nov would sell Volex (5.83%) and Sdiptech (6.41%) in full at Tue 10-Nov; that needs a fall of 17 points in three sessions, after a 20-point weekly fall in the week of 16 to 23-Oct. Arteche and Alfen, already selling, are in the same industry.

The Portfolio Fitness for Fighting assessment of the ideal portfolio (the table of 30-Oct-2026): Overweight Consumer staples and Energy, commodities and metals mining; Double Underweight Consumer discretionary and Real assets; Underweight Defence (moved from Neutral on 30-Oct, on the Defence de-rating) and Media; Neutral elsewhere, Industrials and capital goods included; Momentum Underweight and Fragility Double Underweight (risk-off beneath a steady index, led by credit and the cost of debt). Its logic, in brief: own the defensives with rising estimates, avoid the leveraged and rate-sensitive, and keep cyclical growth only where its own estimates rise. The book is the other way round on size: Consumer staples is about 11.5% of capital (Carlsberg, Fevertree, Hilton Food), against about 21.9% in Industrials and capital goods.

Arteche

Middle Innings Core Investment · 3.96% of capital in 3 tranches (tranche 1, 1.74%, bought 26-Jun-2026; tranche 2, 1.30%, bought 19-Aug-2026; tranche 3, 0.92%, bought 29-Oct-2026) · readings as at Tue 3-Nov-2026

1. SP and MAs

Arteche's SP steadied on Tuesday 3-Nov but did not repair Monday's break: it rose 1.0% to €29.3 after trading up 2.8% to €29.8, closed at 0.38 of the day's range, and is still 5.3% below its rising absolute 200D MA (€30.93). Arteche is up 49.5% over 12 months and 2.4% over 3, down 17.0% over one, and 22.3% below its €37.7 close of 30-Sep and 33.4% below its €44.0 52W closing high (8-May-2026).

The legs since the 30-Sep high, against the industry (Industrials and capital goods, Market chart) and the large grid and electrification names (chart files): 1-Oct, (5.8%), Arteche's own (industry (1.3%)); 6 to 23-Oct, (17.5%), the sector's (Prysmian (24.0%)); 23 to 29-Oct, +5.6%, a bounce Arteche lagged (Prysmian +16.0%); 30-Oct, (3.8%), the sector's (Nexans (7.6%)); 2-Nov, (5.2%), Arteche's own (industry +1.0%, Siemens Energy +2.4%, Legrand +1.4%); 3-Nov, +1.0%, with the sector (industry 0.0%, Stoxx Europe 600 (0.6%), ABB +1.6%, Schneider Electric (0.3%)).

On 3-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "Arteche SP chart very negative L3D. Why is that. Something is happening. Down 15% in 2D. Up 2% today. Why such a big decline". On closes the fall was (8.8%) in two sessions, not 15%: from €31.8 on 29-Oct to €29.0 on 2-Nov. From the 29-Oct intraday high (€32.3) to the 2-Nov intraday low (€28.8) it was (10.8%). That two-session fall is Arteche's fourth largest since June 2025, behind the 23 to 25-Jun-2026 placement (13.1%), 10 to 14-Jul-2026 (9.3%) and 27 to 29-Jul-2026 (9.2%). Your "up 2% today" was intraday: Arteche reached +2.8% on 3-Nov and closed +1.0%.

Developments: Richard's weekly AlphaSense report (4-Nov-2026) has no section on Arteche. The cause of the 30-Oct and 2-Nov falls is not confirmed in the sources read, and no Arteche announcement dated 30-Oct to 3-Nov was found. Two dated items in the research filed bear on the window:

  • The Spanish grid plan's timing (Expert Call, AlphaSense, 30-Oct-2026): the Vice President and Global Head of Grid at Qualitas Energy sees "gradual opportunities for Arteche" in Spain's €17bn grid investment plan, expects final approval "in late 2026 or early 2027", and expects initial tenders to ramp "only from late 2027 through 2030". The Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo (1-Nov) adds that the snap election of 29-Nov-2026 will push Redeia's plan into late 2027, and an expert sizes Spain at €30m to €80m of cumulative Arteche orders through 2030.
  • GE Vernova's agreement to buy Trench (28-Oct-2026; Deutsche Bank, 1-Nov, sizes it at $9bn): Arteche opened 3.2% higher on 29-Oct and has fallen 7.9% since.

Hypothesis 1 (the better supported): the market is unwinding the Spain-specific premium Arteche gained in September. For it: Arteche rose 12.6% from 15 to 18-Sep as Spain announced over €17bn of transmission investment (La Moncloa, 16-Sep), against +1.0% to +5.5% for its peers. The 30-Oct expert call dates the first orders from that plan to late 2027 at the earliest. Arteche's two falls against its sector (1-Oct and 2-Nov) came on its two heaviest volumes of the decline (item 2 (Volume, and relative price against its 52W high)), and its 3-Nov close (€29.3) is below its 15-Sep close (€30.2), the day before the plan was announced. Against it: an expert call is not a broker note or a company statement, no estimate has moved (item 4 (Consensus by named FY)), and the memo already treated Spain as "a slow, modest tailwind". Test: whether Arteche falls further, or more than its sector, on the next Spanish political or regulatory news before the 29-Nov election.

Hypothesis 2: a sector de-rating, amplified in a thin stock (about €0.6m to €1.0m traded a day). For it: on 3-Nov Arteche moved with the sector again. Against it: 2-Nov's fall came on a day the sector rose, on above-average volume. Test: Arteche against Legrand, Siemens Energy and ABB on the next two sector up days.

The MAs: Arteche closed below its 20D MA on 8-Oct, its 100D and 150D MAs on 19-Oct, its 50D and absolute 200D MAs on 22-Oct, back above the absolute 200D MA on 27-Oct, below it on 30-Oct, 6.1% below it on 2-Nov and 5.3% below on 3-Nov. The 20D and 100D MAs are falling; the 50D (€32.32), 150D (€33.07) and absolute 200D MAs are rising. Precedent: Arteche closed 1.9% below the absolute 200D MA at the 1-Sep low; the 2 and 3-Nov closes are its two widest below the line in 2026. Against your 5% principle (your Carlsberg note, 28 to 31-Oct: a price that holds "< 5% below an MA" has "not triggered an exit"; Carlsberg "was testing/holding"), a close at about €29.4 or higher, which is within 5% below the absolute 200D MA (€30.93), would put Arteche back inside that band.

Healthy retest or broken trend? Arteche against your trend-health tests

  • The absolute 200D MA rising: passes (+3.4% over 20 sessions).
  • The 150D MA above the absolute 200D MA: passes (€33.07 against €30.93).
  • The 50D MA above the 150D and absolute 200D MAs: partly (above the absolute 200D MA only).
  • The SP above each MA: fails (9.3% below the 50D, 11.4% below the 150D, 5.3% below the absolute 200D MA).
  • Near its 52W high: fails (33.4% below).
  • Away from its 52W low: passes (48.0% above the €19.8 closing low of 18-Nov-2025).

Your Stage 3 (topping) red flags, counted from the 29-Jul low, after 3-Nov: the largest one-day fall since the advance began, present (1-Oct, (5.8%), on 1.94x volume); the largest one-week fall, present ((8.8%) in the two sessions to 2-Nov); wider swings, partly (average daily range 4.2% of the SP in the last 5 sessions, as in the September advance, against 3.1% in August); lower lows, present but not extended on 3-Nov (3-Nov's low €29.0 against 2-Nov's €28.8); more down days than up days, present (14 against 10 since 30-Sep); a light-volume breakout reversed on heavy volume, partly (the 30-Sep high on 0.76x, reversed on 1.94x); the SP tracking a rising MA instead of bouncing off it, resolved downward on 2-Nov.

Verdict: unchanged by 3-Nov, a broken medium-term trend inside a long-term trend that still passes. What would settle it: a close back above the absolute 200D MA (€30.93) on 1.5x volume within days would make 2-Nov a shakeout; a close below €28.0 (the 1-Sep low) would leave no higher low since July.

2. Volume, and relative price against its 52W high

Basis: ratios leave out the two mechanical-volume days (31-Aug and the 18-Sep index review), with the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's figure in brackets. Arteche's 50D average is 24,100 shares without them and 31,170 with them.

Arteche's 3-Nov rebound came on below-average volume after a 2-Nov fall on above-average volume: the pattern of a decline that has not yet found a buyer. On 3-Nov Arteche rose 1.0% on 21,666 shares (€0.63m), 0.89x (0.69x) its 50D average, opening 0.3% higher and closing at 0.38 of the day's range. On 2-Nov it fell 5.2% on 34,075 shares (€0.99m), 1.41x (1.09x), opening at the day's high and closing at 0.09 of its range.

Heavy-volume days (1.5x or more) in the last 25 sessions: one, 1-Oct. Arteche fell 5.8% on 53,152 shares (€1.89m), 1.94x (1.55x), closing at 0.31 of its range, the session after the 30-Sep high. The 29-Sep accumulation day (+5.0% on 1.75x) has left the 25-session window.

Distribution days on your definition (a down day on 1.25x the 50D average or more, in the last 25 sessions): two on the basis without mechanical-volume days, 1-Oct and 2-Nov, at the start and the end of the decline. The PMS Per-stock Dashboard counts one (1-Oct), because its 50D average includes the 18-Sep index review; its count has been 1 at each weekly sample since 6-Oct. No accumulation day has come in the 25 sessions on either basis.

Up-day against down-day volume: 0.73 over 5 sessions (the dashboard's figure), 0.90 over 20, and 0.58 over the whole decline from 30-Sep (10 up days averaging 18,700 shares, 14 down days 23,000). The May to July 2026 decline ran at 0.64 on the swing table's basis: buyers in this decline are thinner than in the last one.

Volume at the MAs and its trend: the 22-Oct break of the 50D and absolute 200D MAs came on 0.79x (0.61x), the 27-Oct recovery on 0.75x (0.58x), the 30-Oct close back below on 0.60x (0.46x), and the 2-Nov close 6.1% below on 1.41x (1.09x): only the last drew above-average volume. The dashboard's 20D average is 0.54 of its 50D average. The light selling you read positively on 2-Nov ("I read the volume charts L3M positively") is still there; buyers of size are not.

How Arteche's 2026 declines ended: the June to July decline with a 6.1% up day on 2.14x the day after the 29-Jul low, closing at the day's high; the May to June decline quietly, its light-volume rebound (22-Jun, 0.35x) failing into the deeper fall to 29-Jul. The 3-Nov rebound (+1.0% on 0.89x, closing in the bottom half) is so far the May to June pattern.

Arteche's relative price is 37.7% below its 52W high (12-May-2026) and 9.8% below its relative 200D MA, which it crossed below on 22-Oct. It was 38.6% below on 2-Nov, 34.7% at the 30-Oct month-end reading and 21.0% four weeks ago (6-Oct). Relative price confirmed the SP's lower low on 2-Nov (15.95, back at its 3-Sep level) and rose 1.4% on 3-Nov.

Peers' volume on 2 and 3-Nov: on 2-Nov the large names rose on 0.72x to 1.13x their 50D averages while Arteche fell on 1.41x. Pfisterer rose 3.7% on 2.31x, the cohort's heaviest day. On 3-Nov Legrand (+0.7%) traded 1.20x, ABB (+1.6%) 1.13x and Schneider Electric ((0.3%)) 1.12x, against Arteche's 0.89x.

Confirms a turn: an up day on 1.5x the 50D average or more (about 36,150 shares, €1.06m at €29.3), closing in the top third of its range and back above the absolute 200D MA (€30.93). Confirms the decline continues: a close below €28.0 (the 1-Sep low) on more than 24,100 shares.

3. RS

Arteche's Stock RS has stopped falling fast but not turned: 63 on 3-Nov against 64 on 2-Nov and 76 at the 30-Oct month-end, still below the 70 line the Sector RS test reads.

  • Stock RS: 94 (2-Oct), 83 (23-Oct), 76 (30-Oct), 63 (3-Nov): (31) points in a month; 89 three months ago; the lowest reading in the data file's 13 months.
  • Sector RS (Electrical equipment, grid and automation): 75 (2-Oct), 13 (23-Oct), 25 (30-Oct), 24 (3-Nov): below 50 since 16-Oct.
  • Industry RS (Industrials and capital goods): 79 (9-Oct), 46 (23-Oct), 46 (30-Oct), 46 (3-Nov): 16 points above 30 (the previous note's 2-Nov reading of 40 is no longer in the data file).

The order of the turn: sector first (between the 9 and 16-Oct Fridays), industry second (16 to 23-Oct), Arteche last (the last week of October). Since 23-Oct the sector and industry have stabilised (Sector RS moved from 13 to 24, Industry RS stayed at 46) while Arteche's Stock RS fell 20 points: the fall is now Arteche's own, the same message as item 1 (SP and MAs)'s 2-Nov leg.

Against peers: among the sector names the PMS Per-stock Dashboard shows, Arteche's 63 ranks third, behind Landis+Gyr (70) and Cenergy (67), far above Schneider Electric (19) and Siemens Energy (18), because Arteche still holds most of its 12-month gain.

Precedent: the last time Arteche's Stock RS stood this close to 70 (73, 11-Sep), it was back at 96 within two weeks, on Spain's €17bn grid plan (La Moncloa, 16-Sep). The 30-Oct expert call (item 1 (SP and MAs)) makes a second Spanish catalyst of that kind less likely before the 30-Nov month-end. The mechanical drag: as November 2025's closes (€19.6 to €23.2) become the 12-month base, Arteche's 12-month gain at a flat €29.3 narrows from 49.5% to about 26% by early December, which weighs on Stock RS with no further fall. Test: the Fri 6-Nov Stock RS reading.

4. Consensus by named FY

Arteche's consensus has not moved since 30-Sep, and did not move on 3-Nov: the SP has fallen 22.3% on flat estimates. FY2026 EPS (€1.077), FY2027 EPS (€1.333) and FY2028 EPS (€1.56) are unchanged over one month, with three analysts each.

  • Over 3 months the nearer years rose fastest: FY2026 EPS +9.3%, FY2027 +5.4%, FY2028 +1.3%. Over 6 months FY2026 and FY2027 EPS rose 17.5% and 17.6% while FY2028 EPS fell 1.3% and FY2028 EBITDA 1.0%: analysts pulled earnings forward rather than raising the trajectory.
  • Implied growth and margin: EPS +23.8% from FY2026 to FY2027 and +17.0% to FY2028, on revenue growth of 16.6% and 14.9%; the implied EBITDA margin rises from 17.7% (FY2026) to 18.5% and 18.9%. FY2027 EPS rose 34% in 12 months against revenue 7%: the upgrades were margin-led.

On 3-Nov you wrote in your Notion note (Market + FfF): "We cannot own stocks that are not upgrading over N3-6M - its an inviolate rule". Arteche passes that rule on FY2026 and FY2027 over 3 and 6 months, fails it on FY2028 over 6 months, and has had no upgrade at all for one month. The same note says "Almost always, price leads estimates (when a sector is low in range)", and Arteche's sector stands low, Sector RS 24: Arteche's SP has led for five weeks, and the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo (1-Nov) expects estimates to follow it down. It rates cuts of 5% or more to FY2027 and FY2028 consensus at about 60% and 65%, and expects the first FY2027 guidance (24-Mar-2027) at about €112m to €120m of EBITDA against €126.2m consensus. The Sellside Analysts' Memo (3-Nov) says the same: "The upgrade cycle has stopped".

Hypothesis: the SP is discounting the cuts the memo expects, as its SP led the June 2026 cut (FY2027 EPS (2.7%), one of two analysts cutting). Against it: no analyst has cut, and the memo's dated test is in March 2027. Test: the first change to FY2027 consensus, most likely after the Strategic Plan 2030, which the memo says could slip past the 29-Nov election.

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

No analyst has cut anything on Arteche: 3 Buys of 3, and EPS, EBITDA, revenue and PT Net Upgrade Breadth all +33% (one of three raising, none cutting), unchanged on 3-Nov. EPS and EBITDA Net Upgrade Breadth fell from +67% at the 30-Sep month-end to +33% at 31-Oct as a September raise left the counting window; revenue and PT Net Upgrade Breadth have been +33% since 30-Sep. There is no broker cutting to report. There has been no Sell in 24 months, and coverage has varied between 1 and 3 analysts.

The brokers cannot be read directly: an AlphaSense search (filed 3-Nov) for every change between 1-May and 3-Nov-2026 found research from none of the eight houses it names (Santander, JB Capital, Cantor Fitzgerald, GVC Gaesco, Renta 4 Banco, Norbolsa, Alantra and Sabadell), so no broker's stated logic is in the sources read.

Arteche's average PT (€39.11) is 33.5% above the SP (€29.3), wider than at any month-end since August 2024.

  • The PT has followed the SP: it rose 58% in six months, in steps after SP rises (€24.8 to €34.2 over the May and June 2026 month-ends, after the May spike), and has not moved since 29-Sep.
  • Precedent: the earlier gaps above 20% (February and March 2025; August 2026) were followed by SP rises, but opened on rising estimates; this one opened on flat estimates.
  • Hypothesis: the gap is wide because three domestic analysts have not reacted to the de-rating, so the PT will not cushion a further fall. The memo expects "a consensus cut with no downgrade" as international coverage arrives: Net Upgrade Breadth would then turn negative while the ratings stay Buys.
  • A data fault, still open: the daily average PT read €47.0 on 15, 16 and 17-Oct and €39.11 on every other day; no broker note explains it.

For the test of SS Sales or SS PT Net Upgrade Breadth below zero AND Stock RS below 50: with Net Upgrade Breadth at +33% and Stock RS at 63, a sell signal at the 30-Nov month-end needs both a broker cut and a further fall of at least 14 points in Stock RS (from 63 to 49 or lower, below the 50 line).

Early Innings Probing Bet · 3.55% of capital in 3 tranches (tranche 1, 1.41%, bought 29-Jun-2026; tranche 2, 1.81%, bought 5-Aug-2026; tranche 3, 0.33%, bought 7-Aug-2026) · readings as at Tue 3-Nov-2026

1. SP and MAs

Alfen's SP is in a downtrend that has paused, not turned: it is (18.5%) over 3 months and only 4.0% up over 12 months, and the 4.7% bounce from the €9.95 low of 26-Oct has come on light volume, like the July low whose rally failed (item 2 (Volume, and relative price against its 52W high)).

  • The 12-month gain is a round trip: €8.26 (20-Mar) to €19.67 (26-May) and back. Since the high, each low has been lower than the one before: €12.31 (29-Jul), €11.10 (2-Sep), €11.09 (16-Sep) and €9.95 (26-Oct). The highs have fallen with one exception: €14.25 (17-Aug), €12.07 (8-Sep), then €12.67 (22-Sep), above the 8-Sep high but 11.1% below the 17-Aug high.
  • Since the previous run (the 2-Nov close), one session: Alfen rose 0.5% to €10.42 on 3-Nov, closing at 64% of the day's range.

The latest bounce, like the October fall, is the grid sector's, not Alfen's own. Alfen rose 4.7% from 26-Oct to 3-Nov while 14 cohort and sector peers in the chart files rose a median of about 4% (Prysmian 9.0%, Siemens Energy 5.4%, ABB 4.0%; item 9, Peers). From 30-Sep to 26-Oct Alfen fell 18.8%, in line with Schneider Electric (19.3%) and Prysmian (17.0%). Developments. Richard's weekly AlphaSense report of 4-Nov-2026 carries one Alfen Development: the third-quarter 2026 results, published on Wed 4-Nov, the day after the 3-Nov close, so none of it is in the SP readings here (Jefferies Research, 4-Nov):

  • Profit beat, revenue down: adjusted EBITDA fell 43% year on year to €4.0m, against consensus of €2.3m; revenue fell 5% to €99.1m.
  • By business: Smart Grids revenue rose 10% to €54.0m on Dutch grid operators' demand; EV Charging revenue fell 7% to €26.8m on legacy-product phase-outs, with its gross margin down to 40.9% on "competitive pricing pressures"; Energy Storage revenue fell 30% to €18.3m, but its backlog rose to €109m, of which €86m is scheduled for FY2027.
  • Guidance and cash: FY2026 revenue guidance of €425m to €475m was reiterated, not narrowed; third-quarter operating cash flow fell to €1.2m from €8.1m.
  • Strategy: a streamlined EV Charging range aimed at Italy, Spain, Portugal and the United Kingdom; Smart Grids focused on its five fastest-growing segments; storage on utility-scale and mobile projects.
  • Broker: Jefferies reiterated Hold with a PT of €14.00, 34% above the 3-Nov close, and named grid-capacity bottlenecks and nitrogen-permit shortages as the key risks.
  • How it reads against the new Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo (dated 3-Nov, published 4-Nov, written before the update): the memo's tests for this update were revenue against Deutsche Bank's €98.4m and adjusted EBITDA against its €3.0m; Alfen beat both. The memo forecasts second-half 2026 adjusted EBITDA of about €1.9m; the third quarter alone delivered €4.0m, so the memo's trough is deeper than the third quarter showed. The memo's storage-intake and substation-count tests (intake above €40m; substations above 950) cannot be judged: neither figure is in the source read.

On the figures: volume against the 50D average on the volume script's basis, without the mechanical-volume days of 31-Aug and 18-Sep; the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's figure in brackets where it differs.

On each day's own average there is one distribution day in 25 sessions (15-Oct): read the dashboard's 3 as no new selling.

Up-day and down-day volume: balanced and light. The up/down volume ratio is 0.79 over 20 sessions and 0.86 over 5 (the dashboard's figures). After the 29-Jul low (€12.31), no day in the first ten sessions passed 0.8x; the rally came later, on 14, 17 and 20-Aug (1.8x to 3.2x), and the low of each of those three days was broken within three sessions. 4. Consensus by named FY

Alfen's consensus has been flat for a month going into the third-quarter results, after three months of EBITDA cuts that fell hardest on the nearest year. (Alfen's years to December; FactSet means; EUR.)

  • EBITDA over 3 months: FY2026 (12.5%) to €22.15m, FY2027 (6.6%) to €28.69m, FY2028 (4.7%) to €34.37m; over 12 months FY2027 is down 26.3%. Revenue rose 0.7% to 1.1% for each year over 3 months.
  • EPS: FY2026 a loss of €0.149 (it was +€0.1825 at the 31-Jul month-end); FY2027 €0.3545 (13.3% up in 3 months, 23.0% down in 12); FY2028 €0.4986.
  • Implication: analysts defer the recovery rather than abandon it; implied EBITDA margins rise from 4.9% (FY2026) to 5.8% (FY2027) and 6.4% (FY2028), with EBITDA growth of 29.5% and 19.8% on revenue growth of about 8% a year.

What the third quarter implies for FY2026 consensus (the results post-date the 3-Nov readings). Nine-month revenue is €360.6m, so FY2026 revenue guidance (€425m to €475m) needs a fourth quarter of €64m to €114m. The €455.3m FY2026 revenue consensus needs a fourth quarter of €94.7m, which is 4.4% below the third quarter's €99.1m. Nine-month adjusted EBITDA is €20.4m (€16.4m in the first half, €4.0m in the third quarter), so the €22.15m FY2026 EBITDA consensus implies a fourth-quarter adjusted EBITDA of only about €1.75m. Hypothesis: FY2026 EBITDA consensus rises after 4-Nov on the third-quarter beat. Against it: the guidance was not raised, and the new Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo (3-Nov) argues FY2027 EPS of €0.35 is "about 85% too high" because two of the five FY2027 EPS brokers use depreciation of €13.5m to €17m against about €21m underlying. Test: the daily FY2026 EBITDA and FY2027 EPS readings in the week to 10-Nov.

The SP fell with the estimates in August and alone in October (22.1% from 17-Aug to 2-Sep as FY2027 EBITDA fell 10.1%; 18.8% from 30-Sep to 26-Oct with every FY2027 estimate unchanged). On 3-Nov you wrote "Almost always, price moves first": the October fall priced either a cut the third quarter did not deliver, or the sector's de-rating. Data limit: month-end consensus is stored only to 31-Aug-2026; FY2026 EPS rests on 6 analysts.

Alfen's analysts hold no Buy and have not moved: 0 Buys, 6 Holds and 4 Sells of 10 on 3-Nov, unchanged since the 30-Sep month-end. Jefferies' €14.00 (reiterated 4-Nov) is the highest PT named in the sources read.

Alfen trades with the large grid and cable names, and its October fall and its bounce since 26-Oct were about theirs; its cohort has split into two rising small caps and three falling ones. Over 3 and 6 months that is right: Alfen's Stock RS fell from 87 to 30 in July and August while its sector held, and its 3-month fall of 18.5% is the cohort's second-worst after Pfisterer's 21.2%. Your note of 3-Nov: recycle capital "into stocks with positive EPS momentum that are oversold for thematic sector reasons, à la NKT, not Alfen". The data supports the contrast, with one caveat. NKT's one-year-forward EPS consensus rose 5% over three months with revenue Net Upgrade Breadth of +10%, and it is 4.3% below its 50D MA with Stock RS 34; Alfen's FY2027 EPS rose 13.3% over three months, but its FY2027 EBITDA fell 6.6% and the new memo calls the EPS rise "an artefact" of depreciation assumptions. Analysts are moving Xvivo's earnings out to FY2028, the year of the hoped-for United States heart launch. Implied EPS growth from FY2026 to FY2027 has fallen from 73% at the 30-Sep month-end to 44%, while FY2027 to FY2028 has widened from 57% to 66%. On 3-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "We cannot own stocks that are not upgrading over N3-6M - its an inviolate rule". Xvivo fails it on both horizons: FY2027 EPS (14.1%) over three months and (20.7%) over six, with no analyst raising any estimate since September. Xvivo's ratings carry no information: 6 Buys of 6 at every month-end since October 2025, through a 37.0% fall from the 13-Aug high. These ratings follow valuation, not momentum; the Sellside Analysts' Memo of 3-Nov notes that the 100% Buy share has "nowhere to go but down".

Xvivo's average PT, SEK 324.9 (unchanged since 28-Oct), is 66.8% above the SP, the widest gap since the February 2026 month-end (72.2%). The average PT has fallen 6.1% since 29-Sep (from SEK 346.1) and the SP has fallen 13.3% (from SEK 224.6). Precedent: in 2025 the gap widened from 12.4% at the January month-end to 115.0% at August while the SP fell from SEK 466.0 to SEK 167.7, so the gap alone has not marked Xvivo's lows.

Xvivo's Net Upgrade Breadth is negative on every measure, and every analyst who moved cut.

  • EPS: (100%), 6 of 6 cutting, from 0% at the 30-Sep month-end
  • EBITDA: (100%), 7 of 7 cutting, from (29%)
  • Revenue: (86%), 6 of 7 cutting, from 0% at 30-Sep and +83% at the May month-end
  • PT: (57%), 4 of 7 cutting, from (14%) at 30-Sep and +71% at the April and May month-ends

Where analysts are cutting, and why. DNB Carnegie cut FY2027 EPS 4% to SEK 5.7 and FY2028 3% to SEK 8.6 for lower abdominal sales, and held its SEK 330 PT; RedEye sized the largest customer's move to its own lung platform at 5 to 6% of 2027 group sales and moved to a 90% discounted-cash-flow valuation (Investment Analyst's earnings summary, 30-Oct; Sellside Analysts' Memo, 3-Nov). Both houses put 2027 margins below the company's 30% goal. The other cutting brokers are not named in the sources read.

Precedent: in 2026 Xvivo's SP turned before its breadth, up on 3-Mar with revenue Net Upgrade Breadth following at the April month-end, down from 21-Apr with Net Upgrade Breadth following at the June month-end. If the pattern holds, Xvivo's next turn shows first in its SP and volume (item 2 (Volume, and relative price against its 52W high)).

Two hypotheses, ranked by the weight of evidence.

The MAs: Theon closed above its 50D MA once (29-Oct, on 1.70 times) and lost it the next session (30-Oct, on 1.23 times). Theon is 2.0% below the 50D (€30.27), 7.1% below the 100D (€31.92), 7.0% below the 150D (€31.89) and 5.9% below the absolute 200D (€31.53), all four falling over 20 sessions. Theon has had no close above the absolute 200D MA since 23-Sep, which fits your 28-Oct reading of it as "a ceiling not a floor".

Healthy retest or broken trend? Theon against your trend-health tests

  • Absolute 200D MA rising: fails, (0.3%) over 20 sessions. 150D above the absolute 200D: passes narrowly (€31.89 against €31.53), the 150D falling. 50D above both: fails; it crossed below the absolute 200D on 21-Oct.
  • SP above each: fails; above the 10D and 20D only, and 0.5% below the 5D (€29.80). Near its 52W high: fails, 22.6% below €38.32 (14-Aug). Away from its low: passes, 25.8% above €23.58 (26-Nov-2025).
  • Stage 3 (topping) red flags: largest one-day fall since the advance began, absent since 24-Aug ((4.9%)); wider swings, present (average daily move 3.0% over 5 sessions against 1.9% over the 20 before); lower lows, absent since 8-Oct (higher lows €27.92 and €28.64); more down days than up, nearly even since 8-Oct (9 up, 8 down); a light-volume breakout reversed on heavy volume, absent (29-Oct reversed on lighter volume); tracking a rising MA, not applicable.
  • Verdict: a broken trend on the medium-term tests, with a small base of higher lows inside it. Would settle it: a close above €31.92 (the 100D) on 1.5 times the 50D average volume repairs it; a close below €28.64 and then €27.92 ends the base.

Reading: the volume says sellers were exhausted at 8-Oct; the falling MAs and RS say the medium-term trend is still down. A base before a breakout and a rally failing under falling MAs look the same on volume until the price settles it.

4. Consensus by named FY

Analysts keep raising Theon's EBITDA and revenue for every year, while cutting FY2027 and FY2028 EPS: the operating case is upgraded, the shareholder's line beyond this year is not. Since the previous run FY2027 EBITDA rose 0.7%; EPS did not move.

  • EPS (1, 3, 6 and 12 months): FY2026 €1.565, +2.8%, +9.3%, +10.7%, +11.5%; FY2027 €1.653, (1.7%), (1.7%), (0.6%), (3.3%); FY2028 €1.969, (0.8%), (1.8%), +0.9%, +12.1%.
  • EBITDA: FY2026 €162.0m, +1.8%, +3.4%, +4.0%, +11.0%; FY2027 €204.3m, +2.0%, +4.0%, +8.8%, +16.4%; FY2028 €242.6m, +1.8%, +3.9%, +9.3%, +32.7%: the later years rise faster, so analysts are raising the operating trajectory. Revenue: FY2026 €601.4m, flat at the ~€600m guidance; FY2027 +11.7% and FY2028 +19.3% in 12 months.
  • Implied growth: FY2027 over FY2026, revenue +23.8%, EBITDA +26.1%, EPS +5.6%; FY2028 over FY2027, revenue +17.1%, EBITDA +18.7%, EPS +19.1%. The implied EBITDA margin rises from 26.9% to 27.4% and 27.8%. The EBITDA-to-EPS gap sits in FY2027.

What separates EBITDA from EPS in FY2027, from the research filed on 30-Oct and 3-Nov: interest, tax and the Exosens stake.

  • Interest: Deutsche Bank cut adjusted EPS about 5% on 11-Sep for the €325m term loan's interest; the closing of the HGH acquisition slipped to early 2027, so its debt lands in FY2027 (memo 3, filed 3-Nov).
  • Tax: Theon guides a 23% to 24% effective tax rate; its first-half 2026 rate was 19.9% only because the €37.2m Exosens fair-value gain was tax-exempt in Cyprus (memo 2, filed 3-Nov).
  • The Exosens stake: some brokers' FY2026 EPS includes fair-value gains on it (ING €1.79, Marex €1.95, against €1.28 at Deutsche Bank and €1.43 at UBS; FactSet broker detail, 28-Oct). Exosens has fallen 7.3% since 30-Sep; on the €269m paid at €54 a share (memo 1) that is about €22m of fourth-quarter fair-value loss so far (computed), so FY2026 EPS on those brokers' basis can fall on 9-Nov without an operating miss.

On 3-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "We cannot own stocks that are not upgrading over N3-6M - its an inviolate rule". On EPS, Theon fails that rule for the years the market prices: FY2027 EPS is (1.7%) over 3 months and (0.6%) over 6, and FY2028 EPS (1.8%) over 3 months. FY2026 EPS passes (+9.3% and +10.7%), but part of that rise is Exosens gains that the stock's fall is now reversing. On EBITDA and revenue Theon passes for every year.

On 3-Nov you also wrote: "Almost always, price leads estimates (when a sector is low in range)"; Defence's Industry RS has read 13 since 30-Sep. Theon fits: its SP peaked on 14-Aug, while FY2027 EPS consensus was unchanged from the 31-Jul to the 30-Sep month-end (€1.681) and fell only in October, to €1.653. The SP led the EPS cut by about two months.

Test: the 9-Nov Q3 trading update. A book-to-bill of 1.0 or more (the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo's test) followed by FY2027 EPS revisions turning up would show upgrade scope reaching the shareholder.

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

Theon's analysts raise EBITDA and revenue while cutting EPS and PTs: less EPS per euro of EBITDA, and a lower price paid for it, not weaker demand. Since the 30-Oct month-end the daily counts moved both ways.

  • FY2027 EPS Net Upgrade Breadth fell to (25%) (2 raising, 4 cutting, of 8), from (14%) at the 30-Oct month-end and zero at the four month-ends before.
  • FY2027 EBITDA Net Upgrade Breadth rose to +56% (5 raising, none cutting, of 9), from +50%; revenue Net Upgrade Breadth to +33% (3 of 9, none cutting), from +25%.
  • PT Net Upgrade Breadth improved to (11%) (1 raising, 2 cutting, of 9), from (25%) (none raising, 2 cutting, of 8) at 30-Oct. It had fallen or held at every month-end since May, when it was +78%.
  • What changed, a hypothesis: the counts fit one analyst entering them who raised EBITDA, revenue and PT and cut FY2027 EPS; the average PT rose from €39.88 to €40.12, which a ninth PT of about €42 would produce (computed). That analyst is not named in the sources read; Deutsche Bank's reiterated €39 (2-Nov) is not the raise.

The brokers cutting, and their stated logic:

  • ING, 15-Sep: Buy kept; PT cut to €38 from €42.50 on 16.1 times 2027 enterprise value to EBIT, "citing investor sensitivity to leverage" and land-defence peers' multiple falling from 21.6 to 17.2 times, with forecasts raised (earnings review).
  • Deutsche Bank, 11-Sep: Buy and €39 kept, adjusted EPS cut about 5% for interest; an earlier 11% cut, undated in the research, was for the higher tax rate and share-issue dilution (memo 2, filed 3-Nov).
  • The second PT cut and the other FY2027 EPS cuts are not named in the sources read.

Theon's ratings have not followed the SP down (8 Buys, no others, no Sells), so they cannot signal a turn; PTs and Net Upgrade Breadth will. Theon's average PT, €40.12, is 35.3% above the close, against 44.6% at the 8-Oct low. The gap between the average PT and the close reached about 35% twice in the past 12 months (November 2025, June 2026). Each time Theon's SP then rose by about a quarter in six to seven weeks, but PT Net Upgrade Breadth was positive (+20% and +56%); it is now (11%).

Would confirm a turn: one more PT raise with no new cut, taking PT Net Upgrade Breadth to zero or above, after the 9-Nov update. Would refute it: further FY2027 EPS or PT cuts after it.

Theon's four peer checks:

  • SP technicals: Kongsberg Gruppen, Hensoldt, Exail Technologies and MilDef are above their absolute 200D MAs (by 2.3% to 38.7%); Theon (5.9% below), Exosens (7.8%) and Ovzon (18.3%) are not; Theon's absolute 200D MA is falling while Exosens' rises and Hensoldt's is flat.
  • Sell-side estimates: Theon's 3-month EPS revision, (2%), sits with Exail Technologies' (3%) and Ovzon's (12%) at the bottom, against MilDef's +24%, Exosens' +6%, Kongsberg Gruppen's +4% and Hensoldt's +2% (PMS Per-stock Dashboard peer panel).
  • Newsflow: Kongsberg Gruppen rose 13.0% on 4.4 times its 50D average volume on its 29-Oct results and has held the gain; Exosens reported Q3 revenue up 23.5% on 27-Oct and fell 4.8% on 2.25 times (Exosens press release; chart file), a beat sold on heavy volume. Your AlphaSense report has no defence peer item this week.
  • Company guidance: Exosens raised its FY2026 guidance on 17-Sep and reaffirmed it on 27-Oct; Theon raised on 7-Sep. Both raised; both lag the cohort.

Hypothesis: the market prices the night-vision pair, Theon and Exosens, as short-cycle soldier equipment, and pays for long-cycle air defence and electronics instead.

  • For it: Exosens tracks Theon most closely (average correlation 0.55), and Theon holds 9.8% of it and buys its tubes; both lag since 8-Oct despite raised guidance; Kongsberg Gruppen (air defence) is the one peer up since 29-Oct; the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo's counter-case calls soldier-equipment demand "a two-to-three-year replenishment surge that peaks in 2027 to 2029".
  • Against it: Exosens' estimates are rising (+6% in 3 months), and Bernstein (28-Oct) sees armies moving to one night-vision device per soldier.
  • For 9-Nov: the market sold a clean Exosens beat on heavy volume, and a Theon beat may meet the same reception. Test: both night-vision names lagging Hensoldt on a good Defence day (5 and 10-Nov) would mean the market prices the segment, not Theon's debt alone.

What the peers imply for Theon's sell tests: Theon's Stock RS (30) sits with Exosens (38), Hensoldt (39) and Ovzon (24), far from MilDef (97), Kongsberg Gruppen (84) and Exail Technologies (82). A cohort fall like 2-Nov's can take Theon's relative price through its €28.91 line (item 2 (Volume, and relative price against its 52W high)) without any Theon news; a cohort rally lifts Sector RS faster than Theon's Stock RS.

The two sessions to 3-Nov traded below the 23-Oct low intraday and closed high in their range: a first test of the low that held.

  • On Mon 2-Nov Avanza traded down to 343.7, its lowest intraday price since 4-Jun-2026 and below the 23-Oct intraday low of 346.6, and closed at 351.6, in the top 6% of the day's range, on 1.11x its 50D average volume.

The MAs: Avanza sits below its falling 20D (363.67) and 50D (379.61) MAs and its rising 100D (385.32), 150D (373.46) and absolute 200D (361.88) MAs; it has regained only the 5D and 10D.

Healthy retest or broken trend? Avanza against your trend-health tests

  • The absolute 200D MA rising: passes; +0.90% over 20 sessions and +0.21% over 5.
  • The 150D MA above the absolute 200D MA: passes; 373.46 against 361.88.
  • The 50D MA above the 150D MA and the absolute 200D MA: passes today, by 1.6% over the 150D, but at a flat SP the falling 50D crosses below the 150D on about 13-Nov.
  • The SP above each: fails; below the 20D, 50D, 100D, 150D and absolute 200D MAs.
  • Near its 52W high: fails; 12.4% below the 408.3 closing high of 28-Aug.
  • Away from its low: partly; 15.1% above the 52W closing low of 310.48 (24-Feb), 2.2% above the decline's low close.
  • Stage 3 (topping) red flags: lower lows, present (relative price's decline low on 2-Nov); more down days than up days, present (26 against 21 since 28-Aug); the largest one-day and five-session falls since the May advance began, present ((2.8%) on 16 and 19-Oct, and (7.8%) in the five sessions to 19-Oct); a light-volume rally reversed on heavy volume, present (12-Oct on 0.65x, reversed 13-Oct on 2.22x); wider swings, results fortnight only; the SP tracking a rising MA rather than bouncing off it, not present (it broke the MAs and has not ridden them).
  • Verdict: a pullback that has broken its medium-term MAs inside a long-term uptrend that is intact but weakening, closer to a broken trend than a healthy retest. What settles it: a heavy-volume close above 361.88 makes it a retest of the absolute 200D MA; the 50D crossing below the 150D near 13-Nov without that close removes a third test.

On 3-Nov you wrote in your Notion note (timed 16:00): "Avanza also a negative SP chart L1W… Stock below 200D MA. SP not recovering L1W". Confirmed in part. Avanza is below its absolute 200D MA (1.2% at the 3-Nov close, 12 closes running), and its recovery lags its peers (item 9 (Peers)). At the close Avanza was 0.8% above its 5D MA and in the top 13% of the day's range.

Avanza's selling has dried up: no session has reached 1.5x its 50D average volume since 21-Oct, nine sessions, and the distribution-day count has fallen from 8 to 6. Buying has not replaced it.

  • The heavy-volume days of the last 25 sessions (volume against the 50D average, mechanical-volume days left out) all fall between 1 and 21-Oct: 1-Oct +0.2% on 1.97x (an accumulation day); 6-Oct (2.5%) on 1.72x, closing at the day's low; 13-Oct (1.4%) on 2.22x; 14-Oct (1.2%) on 1.79x; 15-Oct +0.2% on 1.50x; 16-Oct, the results day, (2.8%) on 4.24x (1,058,139 shares, SEK 387m), gapping 3.8% lower and closing in the top 30% of the range; 19-Oct (2.8%) on 1.99x (SEK 180m), closing in the lowest quarter, below the absolute 200D MA; 20-Oct (0.2%) on 1.68x; 21-Oct +1.6% on 1.56x (SEK 149m), an accumulation day.

Up-day against down-day volume: 0.58 over 20 sessions, 0.90 over 5 (the dashboard's figures); over the whole decline from 28-Aug, 0.77, with 7 heavy-volume down days against 3 heavy up days.

  • That 0.77 equals February's decline (0.77) and is below April to May's (0.94) and October 2025 to January's (1.01): level with February's as the heaviest selling of Avanza's four declines in 12 months.

Precedent: Avanza's February and May 2026 lows turned on runs of up days at 0.5x to 0.7x average volume; January's on +1.3% at 1.6x. The current bounce matches the light-volume pattern in its volume but not in its pace (item 1 (SP and MAs): +2.2% in seven sessions, against +4.2% to +9.4%). Confirms a turn: an up day on 1.5x or more, closing in the top third of its range and above the absolute 200D MA. Confirms the decline continues: a close below 349.7 on above-average volume, the first new distribution day since 20-Oct.

Avanza's Stock RS rose to 35 on 3-Nov from 30 on 2-Nov (the previous note's reading), the first rise since 2-Oct; it is still 35 points below the 70 line and 15 below the 50 line. Sector RS (19) is at the file's lows and Industry RS (59) is unchanged.

Sector RS fell 25 points from 24-Jul to 25-Sep while Avanza's Stock RS held 52 to 73; Avanza's then fell 12 points in the results week (51 to 39, 9 to 16-Oct). Avanza's Stock RS is 20 points below Nordnet's (55), from 9 points a month ago (60 against 69): the stock's own underperformance is widening inside a weak sector.

Precedent: Avanza's Stock RS was last this low on 8-May-2026 (33), near the 13-May SP low; it rose 14 points to 47 by the 29-May month-end, one point short of the 50 line, so the test of SS Sales or SS PT Net Upgrade Breadth below zero AND Stock RS below 50 still gave a sell signal then. 4. Consensus by named FY

Avanza's consensus is being cut at the near year and lifted at the far one: FY2026 EPS (1.0%) and FY2027 (0.3%) over the month to 3-Nov, FY2028 +1.1%.

  • EPS: FY2026 19.34 (1M (1.0%), 3M (0.1%), 6M +6.6%, 12M +15.7%); FY2027 21.01 (1M (0.3%), 3M +1.4%, 6M +7.2%, 12M +11.7%); FY2028 23.24 (1M +1.1%, 3M +2.6%, 6M +8.5%, 12M +10.5%).
  • Revenue: FY2026 SEK 5,197m (1M (1.0%)), FY2027 SEK 5,734m ((0.2%)), FY2028 SEK 6,315m (+0.6%). Avanza, a bank, has no EBITDA consensus.
  • Implied EPS growth: FY2026 to FY2027 8.6% and FY2027 to FY2028 10.6%, from 7.9% and 9.1% a month ago. Analysts are pushing the trajectory out, not pulling it forward, which fits the memo's reading that 2027 carries the Danish launch costs and 2028 the benefit of a higher policy rate.

The link: Deutsche Bank's 6% cut (30-Oct) moved the mean little because most houses have not yet updated. The Sellside Analysts' Memo of 3-Nov counts only three houses publishing since the 16-Oct print, ten still on pre-print numbers, and expects FY2027 consensus to fall 3% to 5% over 18 months, with the 22-Jan-2027 results the first test. Hypothesis: the SP has priced part of the cut first (SP (10.0%) over 3 months, FY2027 EPS +1.4%). Test: FY2027 EPS through the mid-January consensus compilation; a 3% cut takes it to about 20.38.

On 3-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "We cannot own stocks that are not upgrading over N3-6M - its an inviolate rule". Avanza's FY2027 EPS is still up over 3 and 6 months (+1.4% and +7.2%), but its EPS Net Upgrade Breadth is negative ((11%)), and the Sellside Analysts' Memo expects cuts: it is moving from passing that rule to failing it.

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

Avanza's Net Upgrade Breadth is negative on every measure: EPS (11%) (6 raising, 8 cutting, of 19), revenue (19%) (5 and 8, of 16) and PT (21%) (5 and 9, of 19), from +90%, +88% and +79% at the August month-end; all three worsened since the previous run, whose latest readings (31-Oct and 1-Nov) were EPS 0%, revenue 0% and PT (11%).

  • The month-end readings that the test of SS Sales or SS PT Net Upgrade Breadth below zero AND Stock RS below 50 uses (100-day count, 30-Oct): sales (6%), PT (16%), from +22% and +24% at 30-Sep.
  • The brokers cutting, and their stated logic: Deutsche Bank (30-Oct), EPS 2026 to 2028 down 6% on average and PT SEK 420 from 440, Buy kept, on "a softer net interest income outlook and cautious trading assumptions"; Nordea (19-Oct), PT SEK 435 from 465, Buy; DNB Carnegie (19-Oct), PT SEK 425 from 430, Buy. Jefferies stays Underperform (30-Oct), on the premium multiple against digital savings peers. The common thread is lower revenue per customer: trading velocity and the interest margin, not customer or savings growth, which keep rising.
  • The cuts come from Buy-rated houses trimming, not from downgrades: ratings are 10 Buy, 5 other and 4 Sell (3-Nov), against 11, 6 and 4 at 30-Sep.

Average PT SEK 409.9 (3-Nov), (2.6%) from 421.0 on 15-Oct; its gap over the SP is 14.7%, from 16.4% on 27-Oct. The gap narrowed mostly because the SP rose (+0.8% from the 27-Oct close), and partly because the average PT fell 0.7% (412.6 on 24-Oct to 409.9). It was widest at the February and April 2026 month-ends (19.4% and 18.6%), each near an SP low.

Hypothesis: Avanza's ratings follow the SP and its PTs follow the estimates. Buys rose from 2 of 14 (October 2024) to 11 of 21 (September 2026) as the SP rose. Precedent: PT breadth was negative at the January, February and May 2026 month-ends, each near an SP low, and the SP rose afterwards. What differs: those were single-month dips inside rising estimates; the Sellside Analysts' Memo now expects two more rounds of cuts.

9. Peers

Avanza is the laggard of its cohort's bounce: since the 23-Oct low Avanza is up 2.2%, against Nordnet +4.6%, Fineco +3.8%, Integrafin +4.2% and Flatex +7.5%; only Swissquote ((1.7%)) and AJ Bell (+0.6%) did worse. The table on the PMS Per-stock Dashboard shows the levels.

  • SP technicals: Nordnet, Avanza's closest peer (correlation 0.68), is 2.6% above its absolute 200D MA, Avanza 1.2% below. Fineco (0.6%) and Avanza are the two just below; Swissquote (13.4%) and Flatex (27.5%) are far below. The United Kingdom platforms, AJ Bell (+7.3%) and Integrafin (+8.6%), are above theirs.
  • Sell-side estimates: Avanza's revenue Net Upgrade Breadth ((19%)) is the cohort's second-worst, ahead only of Flatex ((36%)); Nordnet (+6%), Fineco (+7%) and AJ Bell (+54%) are positive. Over 3 months FY2027 EPS is rising at Avanza, Nordnet and Fineco (+1% to +2%) and falling at Swissquote ((3%)) and Flatex ((4%)).
  • Newsflow and company guidance: the AlphaSense report's peers section covers none of Avanza's cohort this week; no peer guidance change is in the sources read. Nordnet's third quarter (22-Oct) was a 2% miss it absorbed (per the Sellside Analysts' Memo).

Who turned first: the sector fell from July with Flatex leading; Avanza's Stock RS fell hardest in its results week, and in the bounce it trails Nordnet on both price and volume (3-Nov: Nordnet +2.1% on 1.32x, Avanza +1.7% on 0.82x). Hypothesis: buyers returning to the Swedish platforms prefer Nordnet, whose estimates are still rising. For: the breadth gap (Nordnet +6%, Avanza (19%)) and the widening Stock RS gap (20 points). Against: Avanza's 3-Nov relative gain was its largest since 3-Sep. Test: Avanza's October statistics (4-Nov) against Nordnet's when it publishes; a narrowing of the gap since 23-Oct by the 6-Nov Friday reading.

What the peers imply: Sector RS (19) is held down by members at Stock RS 3 (Flatex), 4 (IG Group), 7 (Swissquote) and 10 (Plus500), so a recovery in Avanza and Nordnet alone would not lift Sector RS the 31 points that the test of Sector RS below 50 with Stock RS below 70 needs to lapse by 30-Nov.

What the rules say for Avanza now, and what decides the next step

  • The finding that matters most: the rules already sell Avanza. Two whole-holding Sell Criteria gave sell signals at the 30-Oct month-end, and under your one-off in/out link it is sold when its replacement is bought, in the Assistant Portfolio Manager's order of sale. Nothing in the one session since 2-Nov changes that.
  • The second: the sell-side is cutting at the near year (FY2026 EPS (1.0%) in a month, all three Net Upgrade Breadths negative), and the brokers cutting name revenue per customer. Your 3-Nov rule, never to own a stock that is not upgrading over 3 to 6 months, is turning against Avanza.
  • The third: the price may be basing. Two tests of the 23-Oct low (2 and 3-Nov) held with closes high in their range, selling volume has dried up for nine sessions, and relative price had its best day since 3-Sep. But the bounce is the weakest of Avanza's four post-low starts, on light volume, and trails Nordnet's.
  • What the rules say for each tranche today: tranche 1 (0.46% of capital now, 0.50% at entry) and tranche 2 (2.51% now, 2.57% at entry) are held until a replacement is bought, then sold with the whole holding. No tranche is near its 30% level, and no tranche sale is due at the 10-Nov session.
  • Sector RS below 50 with Stock RS below 70 (sell signal at 30-Oct): Sector RS fell from 68 (30-Sep) to 17 (30-Oct) and reads 19; Stock RS from 56 to 33 and reads 35. To lapse at 30-Nov, Sector RS must rise 31 points or Stock RS 35. Sector RS rose 35 points in March 2026, so it has happened once in the file.
  • SS Sales or SS PT Net Upgrade Breadth below zero AND Stock RS below 50 (sell signal at 30-Oct): to lapse at 30-Nov, Stock RS must rise 15 points to 50, or both breadths return to zero or above. The rise in Stock RS is the likelier path, since the Sellside Analysts' Memo expects more cuts as the ten pre-print houses update.
  • The 200D MA of relative price flat or falling over the month (next read Fri 6-Nov): +0.17% over the month on 3-Nov, from +0.25% on 27-Oct. At a flat relative price it reads about +0.11% on 6-Nov and (0.15%) on 13-Nov, a sell signal for a Core Investment. To give it on 6-Nov, relative price would have to fall about 8% in three sessions. A third whole-holding sell signal would not change when Avanza sells under the one-off in/out link.
  • Relative price more than 25% below its 52W high (next read 30-Nov): 14.7% below now, from 15.4% at 30-Oct. At a flat market the line is SEK 314.29, 12.1% below the 3-Nov close; February 2026's furthest point was 21.8%.
  • How the tests interact: a fall of about 12% at a flat market would trip the relative 52W-high test at 30-Nov and confirm the relative 200D MA test from 13-Nov. A further 9.1% beyond that (20.1% in all from the 3-Nov close) would trigger tranche 1's 30% fall at SEK 285.81. That trigger sells at the Tuesday session after its release even under the link, because Stock RS (35) is below 70.
  • The record: the test of SS Sales or SS PT Net Upgrade Breadth below zero AND Stock RS below 50 would also have given a sell signal at the January, February and May 2026 month-ends (Stock RS 33, 23 and 47; SS PT Net Upgrade Breadth (12%), (12%) and (6%)), but the test of Sector RS below 50 with Stock RS below 70 only at February's (Sector RS 38, against 74 in January and 85 in May). Selling at the following Tuesday sessions (3-Feb at 341.42, 3-Mar at 321.08, 2-Jun at 339.2) would have avoided a 9.1% fall after January (to 310.48 on 24-Feb) but missed rises of 21% after February (to 389.62 on 15-Apr) and 20% after May (to 408.3 on 28-Aug). What differs now: both tests give a sell signal together, as only in February; Sector RS is far weaker (17 against 38 to 85 then), and estimates are being cut rather than raised.
  • Next reading day and adds: Fri 6-Nov reads Industry RS (59, far from 30) and the relative 200D MA (about +0.11% at a flat relative price). The rules block an add, because two Sell Criteria give sell signals. Where Avanza stands for an add: none of the three stages (not pulling back or basing, not retesting a rising MA or forming a volatility contraction pattern, not healthily retesting or breaking out). The Master Dashboard's own tests read Pulling Back 1 of 4, Basing 2 of 4 (possible), Healthy Retest 3 of 6 (possible, the absolute 200D MA retested from 1.21% below) and both volatility contraction patterns 0 of 4. Neither add trigger is present.

Richard decides.

Bally's Intralot

Middle Innings Core Investment · 1.92% of capital in 1 tranche (tranche 1, 1.92%, bought 26-Jun-2026) · readings as at Tue 3-Nov-2026

One session since the previous note (2-Nov close). Closes, legs and MAs use the data file's unrounded closes on Athens trading days (the chart file rounds Bally's Intralot to €0.01, about 0.9%); period returns are the working file's unless stated.

1. SP and MAs

Bally's Intralot is still flat while its gambling peers rally, and the one new fact is that the company itself is buying its shares at the base's floor. It closed at €1.127 on 3-Nov, up 0.6% on the session and 0.7% over five sessions (from €1.119 on 26-Oct) while the Stoxx Europe 600 rose 0.8% on the same span; at a beta of 0.67 the market explains 0.5 points of the 0.7% rise (0.67 times 0.8%), so the part that is Bally's Intralot's own was +0.2 points, 0.1 times its usual weekly move of 3.6%.

  • On 3-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "Bally's SP flat L1W. Not doing much". Confirmed: +0.7% over five sessions on unrounded closes, and every close since 23-Oct between €1.117 and €1.127.
  • The trend: (1.7%) over 3 months (Stoxx Europe 600 (1.9%)) and +5.0% over 12 months (from €1.074 on 24-Oct-2025), in a year spent between €0.831 (30-Mar) and €1.197 (23-Jun). Since tranche 1 was bought on 26-Jun at €1.180, the SP is down 4.5% and relative price 8.7%, which on the 2.0% of capital at entry is about 0.09 points of capital in absolute terms and 0.17 points relative: the answer, in numbers, to your 31-Oct question "It is only 2.0% of capital so largely irrelevant… But what's the point…?".
  • The legs since the August high: €1.176 (14-Aug) to €1.059 (18-Sep), (9.9%); to €1.140 (1-Oct), +7.6%; to €1.080 (8-Oct), (5.3%); to €1.130 (21-Oct), +4.6%; to €1.127 (3-Nov), (0.3%): rising lows and falling highs, a narrowing triangle.

Developments (AlphaSense weekly report of 4-Nov, covering about 28-Oct to 4-Nov; read in full):

  • 2-Nov, the share buyback through Deutsche Bank's total return equity swap (press release): 490,000 shares on 26-Oct and 465,000 on 30-Oct at an average €1.11 (€1.06m in all), lifting Deutsche Bank's holding to 21.17M shares, 1.13% of the share capital; on settlement the shares return to Bally's Intralot as own shares. The buyback sits beside the flat closes of 26 to 30-Oct (item 2 (Volume, and relative price against its 52W high) measures its share of the volume). The Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo counts "a buyback at that leverage" (4.05x) among its three red flags.
  • Not in the report: Bally's Corporation, the parent, whose SP fell 3.8% on 3-Nov for a cause not in the sources read (item 9 (Peers)).
  • The flat leg since 21-Oct is Bally's Intralot's own: Consumer discretionary rose 1.9% and the online peers 6% to 9% in the week. Hypothesis: the base is held from below by the company's swap buying (item 2 (Volume, and relative price against its 52W high)) and capped from above by the pending issue of new shares to Evoke's holders, about 11.5% of the enlarged group, and by the parent's liquidity strain (item 9 (Peers)). Test: the SP's reaction to the swap's next disclosure and to Bally's Corporation's third-quarter 10-Q, expected in November.

The MAs: the long MAs rise, the medium ones fall, and the SP sits on all of them, unchanged in shape since 2-Nov. On 3-Nov: 100D €1.123 (falling 0.6% over 21 sessions), 20D €1.113 (rising 2.0%), 150D €1.111 (rising 2.9%), 50D €1.106 (falling 0.9%), absolute 200D €1.071 (rising 0.7%). The SP closed back above the 100D MA on 3-Nov (0.3% above) on 0.57x the 50D average volume, a crossing without conviction; it is 5.3% above the absolute 200D MA, which held at the 23-Jul and 18-Sep turns.

Healthy retest or broken trend? Bally's Intralot against your trend-health tests (a base, not a pullback; as on 2-Nov except the SP is now above every MA):

  • The absolute 200D MA rising; the 150D MA above the absolute 200D MA; the SP above each MA: pass (the 100D by 0.3%).

2. Volume, and relative price against its 52W high

Bally's Intralot's chart file rounds prices to €0.01 (about 0.9%), so changes and closes below use the data file's unrounded closes; close-in-range readings near 0 or 1 are coarse. The 50D average holds two mechanical-volume days (31-Aug; 18-Sep, an index day), which lower each ratio by about 10%; the volume script's figure without them is in brackets.

Part of the demand holding Bally's Intralot's base at €1.11 to €1.13 is the company's own buyback. Bally's Intralot announced on 2-Nov (press release, in the AlphaSense weekly report of 4-Nov) that Deutsche Bank bought 955,000 shares at an average €1.11 under the total return equity swap of 16-Jun-2026, which settles by delivering the shares back to the company as own shares.

  • The share of the tape: Deutsche Bank's 490,000 shares on 26-Oct were 22.5% of that day's 2.17M shares, and its 465,000 on 30-Oct were 28.4% of 1.64M; over the four Athens sessions from 26 to 30-Oct (Athens was shut on 28-Oct for Ohi Day) the swap bought 12.4% of the 7.72M shares traded.
  • The price: every close since 23-Oct has sat between €1.117 and €1.127, just above the swap's €1.11 average, and the chart file's daily low was €1.11 in every session from 27-Oct to 3-Nov (€1.10 on 26-Oct).
  • The capacity: the swap is capped at €50m over 12 months (AlphaSense research reports filed 2 and 3-Nov); Deutsche Bank's 21.17M shares are worth about €24m at €1.11, so on my estimate about half the cap may be used (the release does not say how many were bought under the swap).
  • Hypothesis: the issuer's bid, not institutional accumulation, is setting the floor of the base. For it: the swap bought a quarter of the volume on its two days, at the base's floor, while the 20D average volume stands at 0.75 times the 50D average (the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's figure). Against it: only two days are disclosed, and the swap did not buy on 29-Oct, the heaviest session of the ten (3.40M shares, €3.81m, 1.31x; 1.43x). Test: the next swap disclosure, and whether the SP holds €1.11 on a week the swap does not buy.

No heavy-volume day since 15-Oct, and volume is still drying up.

  • Heavy-volume days in 25 sessions (1.5x the 50D average or more): two up days, unchanged since 2-Nov: 29-Sep, +2.7% on 5.23M shares (€5.91m, 1.94x; 2.06x), and 15-Oct, +2.1% on 6.61M shares (€7.34m, 2.36x; 2.54x).

The online peers rallied a second week and Lottomatica's results backed the consolidation story; Bally's Intralot, the consolidator buying Evoke, did not move. The peer table on the PMS Per-stock Dashboard carries the figures; these are the points they make.

  • SP technicals: over the week to 3-Nov Evoke rose 9.3%, Lottomatica 8.7%, Flutter 7.0% and Entain 5.8%, against Bally's Intralot's 0.9%. Flutter fell 4.5% on 1.86x on 3-Nov after rising 6.1% on 2-Nov, for a cause not in the sources read.
  • Newsflow and company guidance: Lottomatica's third quarter (3-Nov, press release and Rothschild & Co Redburn) showed normalised revenue growth of 12% and FY2026 normalised adjusted EBITDA expected at the top end of guidance, and Morgan Stanley (3-Nov) noted that it continues to gain share from smaller tail operators, whose Italian online share fell to 11.6% in the third quarter. That is the concentration your Sector Digest says a duty rise produces, which is the opening that Bally's Intralot's purchase of Evoke is built on in the UK.
  • Sell-side estimates: even the best peer is not upgrading on earnings: Rothschild & Co Redburn raised its Lottomatica PT to €32.50 from €31.50 (4-Nov, after these readings) while trimming its 2026 to 2028 estimates, and Morgan Stanley trimmed FY2026 (3-Nov); Lottomatica's 3-month EPS revision is (1%). Only Betsson has rising estimates (3-month EPS +4%, revenue +6%). What it suggests: the sector's rally is price leading estimates, as your 3-Nov note expects ("Almost always, price moves first"); Bally's Intralot has neither.

The parent fell 3.8% on 3-Nov and Bally's Intralot rose 0.6%: the subsidiary did not follow its parent down. Bally's Corporation, the 58% owner, closed at $13.00, its Stock RS 18 against 70 a month ago. The AlphaSense research report filed 3-Nov summarises the parent's filings: a conditional covenant waiver through the first quarter of 2027, forecasts showing "a high probability of failing the minimum liquidity maintenance test", and the third-quarter 10-Q still to come in November. Hypothesis: parent stress is an overhang on Bally's Intralot (cash extraction, a forced sale of shares), not a daily driver: the 14-Aug going-concern disclosure started the (9.9%) leg to 18-Sep, but 3-Nov's moves split. Test: the 10-Q's disclosures on intercompany balances and any sale of Bally's Intralot shares.

Evoke's deal spread is about 10%, little changed in the session. Bally's Intralot is buying Evoke for 0.537 new Bally's Intralot shares per Evoke share. On my estimate at the 23-Oct exchange rate (the rate since is not in the sources read), the terms are worth about 52.3p at €1.127; Evoke closed at 47.0p on 3-Nov, about 10% below, against about 9% on 2-Nov and 17% on 27-Oct. The narrowing brings nearer the day Evoke's holders receive new Bally's Intralot shares, which they can sell.

What the peers imply: the sector's turn is under way without Bally's Intralot, whose daily moves correlate only weakly with any stock (CrediaBank, 0.24): a stock with its own overhangs (leverage, the parent, the Evoke share issue), not one waiting for its industry.

2. Volume, and relative price against its 52W high

Multiples are the volume script's (shares against the 50D average); the mechanical-volume days of 31-Aug and 18-Sep move none by more than 0.01x. For 2-Nov the dashboard's value basis gives 1.55x against the script's 1.41x.

3-Nov was a quiet test of the breakout level that held: CVS Group gapped down 0.8% to 1,349p, traded down to 1,321p, 7p below the 7-Sep high, and closed at 1,367p, 0.72 of a 1,321p to 1,385p range, on 0.97x its 50D average (346,162 shares, £4.7m). The 1,385p intraday high was CVS Group's highest since 25-Feb-2026. The day was CVS Group's own (Stoxx Europe 600 (0.6%), Healthcare +0.1%). Hypothesis: fewer sellers wait at the old high than in October. What would refute it: a close back below 1,328p on 1.5x volume or more.

CVS Group's heavy days have turned from selling to two-way trade. Of six heavy days (1.5x or more) in 25 sessions, the two at the low were down days (30-Sep, (1.7%) on 3.84x, 1,205,027 shares, £14.7m, the quarter-end; 2-Oct, (1.7%) on 1.57x, £5.5m). Since then two up (20-Oct, +2.6% on 1.85x, £7.7m, closing at 0.93; 30-Oct, +0.9% on 1.52x, £7.0m) and two down (21-Oct, (1.5%) on 1.68x, £7.0m, closing at 0.04; 26-Oct, (1.0%) on 1.88x, £8.1m, after a 1.0% gap up), both down days at 1,300p to 1,323p under the 7-Sep high, with no cause in the sources read.

CVS Group's distribution days in the latest 25 sessions have fallen from 7 to 4 in a week (30-Sep, 2-Oct, 21 and 26-Oct), and without a new one they number 2 by Fri 6-Nov, as 30-Sep and 2-Oct leave the 25-session window. Accumulation days number 5 (5, 14, 20 and 30-Oct, 2-Nov).

Up-day volume leads over 5 sessions and trails over 20, and the advance from the 2-Oct low is thin by CVS Group's own standards.

  • The dashboard's up/down volume ratio is 1.49 over 5 sessions and 0.82 over 20; the 20D average volume is 1.07 times the 50D.
  • From 1,140p to 1,367p, +19.9% in 22 sessions, CVS Group had 16 up days and 6 down days, but the average up day traded 0.89 times the average down day.
  • Precedent: of seven advances of 8% or more in 12 months only two were thinner, and both ended in sharp falls: July to September 2026 (0.70), then (14.2%); July to October 2025 (0.85), then (20.6%). The four others ran at 1.07 to 2.01. Hypothesis: the advance has rested more on sellers withdrawing than on heavy buying; the 5-session ratio is the first sign of that changing.

The buyback buys a steady 5% to 8% of CVS Group's volume, and its end is near. CVS Group bought 134,615 shares from 26 to 30-Oct (CVS Group, 2-Nov), 6.7% of that week's 1,998,580 shares on my arithmetic. The 3,467,465 shares bought at an average 1,250.8p cost about £43.4m of the up to £50m programme, leaving about £6.6m, three to four weeks at about £1.8m a week. Implication: a steady buyer leaves around the end of November, the weeks of the annual general meeting (25-Nov) and the 30-Nov readings.

Volume at the MAs, and how declines ended: CVS Group regained its MAs on 14 and 16-Oct on 1.33x and 1.08x, reversing the 24-Sep break of four MAs on 2.77x. After the 2-Oct low, as after the March, May and June 2026 lows, volume faded (one 1.49x accumulation day on 5-Oct, then 0.48x to 1.14x for six sessions); the 17-Nov-2025 low instead ended on a 3.3x day on the annual general meeting trading update.

CVS Group's relative price has not confirmed the SP's high: the SP is 3.5% below its 52W closing high, relative price 10.5% below its own (841.7, 17-Feb-2026). Relative price (753.6) is at its highest since 19-Mar-2026, the SP since 25-Feb-2026, because the market has risen since February. Relative price has closed above its relative 200D MA since 22-Oct, held it on 26 and 29-Oct (0.1% and 0.03% above) and is now 4.3% above it.

Peers' volume: on CVS Group's heavy days cohort peers traded heavily mostly on small or opposite moves; on 3-Nov Medicover fell 1.4% on 1.52x.

Confirms the turn: a close above 1,416p on 1.5x the 50D average or more, in the top third of the range. Confirms a failure: a close below 1,328p on heavy volume, or a new distribution day after the buyback ends.

3. RS

CVS Group's three RS readings rose again on 3-Nov, and CVS Group's Stock RS of 61 is its highest since 20-Feb-2026 (63).

  • Stock RS: 61 on 3-Nov, from 60 on 2-Nov, 58 at the 30-Oct month-end, 32 on 16-Oct and 25 on 9-Oct; it was 45 three months ago (4-Aug) and 61 twelve months ago (7-Nov-2025). It is 11 points above the line of 50 and 9 below the line of 70.
  • Sector RS: the Sector RS of Hospitals, clinics and care homes is 68, from 67 on 2-Nov, 66 at 30-Oct, 58 on 23-Oct and 19 on 16-Oct; it is 18 points above its line of 50.
  • Industry RS: Healthcare's Industry RS is 86, unchanged since 30-Oct and the highest in 13 months of readings, from 46 on 16-Oct; it is 56 points above its line of 30.

The order of the turn: CVS Group first (Stock RS 25 to 32 in the week to 16-Oct, as its Sector RS fell from 31 to 19), then Healthcare and the sector together in the week to 23-Oct (Industry RS 46 to 79, Sector RS 19 to 58). The rotation has carried CVS Group since 23-Oct; its 2 and 3-Nov gains were its own.

The Sector RS has now read 50 or more at every reading since 23-Oct, including the Fri 23-Oct and the 30-Oct month-end readings, which meets the Sector Digest's own test (28-Oct: "wait for a second reading") that a Sector RS above 50 in this sector is more than a one-week peak; after 7-Aug and 18-Sep it fell below 50 within a week.

The precedents are warnings. CVS Group's Stock RS stood at 82 to 84 with a Sector RS of 82 to 85 in October 2025, after the Competition and Markets Authority's provisional decision; CVS Group then fell 20.6% in 17 sessions to 17-Nov-2025 and its Stock RS dropped from 61 to 47 in the week to 14-Nov. Its Stock RS read 60 or more from 19-Dec-2025 to 16-Jan-2026 (64 to 71), dropped to 51 at the 30-Jan month-end while the SP dipped 5.1% to 1,306p and then made a new high; its return to 68 and 63 on 13 and 20-Feb-2026 ended in a 21.3% fall to 30-Mar and a Stock RS of 31 at the 31-Mar month-end. The difference now: Healthcare's Industry RS is 86, against 26 to 59 in both earlier runs.

What would confirm it: Stock RS above 61 and Industry RS above 70 on Fri 6-Nov. What would refute it: Stock RS back below 50, as it fell 17 points in the week to 25-Sep.

4. Consensus by named FY

CVS Group's FY2027 is its year to June 2027; FactSet consensus in pence and £m, to 3-Nov-2026.

CVS Group has risen 18.9% in a month with its estimates almost flat: price leads, as your 3-Nov market note expects ("Almost always, price leads estimates (when a sector is low in range)"), but the estimates have not yet followed.

  • FY2027: EPS 95.5p, +1.0% over 1 month, (0.1%) over 3 months, +0.6% over 6 and (0.2%) over 12, and (10.8%) since October 2024; revenue £751.0m and EBITDA £150.8m moved 0.6% or less over 1, 3 and 6 months.
  • FY2028: EPS 103.8p, +0.6% over 1 month, (1.0%) over 3 months, (0.9%) over 6, (1.7%) over 12.
  • FY2029: EPS 112.9p, +1.5% over 1 month, (3.5%) over 3 months, (1.4%) over 6; EBITDA £167.6m, (0.8%) over 3 months and (3.6%) over 6.

The cutting of the later years stopped in October, and 3-Nov brought the first upgrade in the outer year, with two more analysts counted. From 31-Oct to 3-Nov FY2027 EPS rose 0.3%, FY2028 0.4% and FY2029 1.4% (FY2029 EBITDA +1.3%, revenue +0.7%), while the number of analysts with an FY2027 EPS estimate rose from 10 to 12. Hypothesis: new or returning coverage with above-average outer-year estimates, not upgrades by existing analysts; it fits the new non-Buy rating and lower average PT the same day (items 5 and 6 (Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth), Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth). The sources read name no new analyst.

The trajectory: EPS grows about 1.6 times as fast as EBITDA, which is the buyback at work. Implied EPS growth is 8.7% from FY2027 to FY2028 and 8.8% to FY2029; revenue 4.4% and 4.8%; EBITDA 5.3% and 5.6%. The implied EBITDA margin is 20.1%, 20.2% and 20.4%, against 19.9% in FY2026 and the 19% to 23% target: analysts expect almost no margin gain.

Against your rule: your 3-Nov market note says "We cannot own stocks that are not upgrading over N3-6M - its an inviolate rule". On that test CVS Group fails today: FY2027 EPS is (0.1%) over 3 months and +0.6% over 6, FY2028 and FY2029 are lower over both, and EPS Net Upgrade Breadth has been zero or negative at 11 of the last 12 month-ends.

The link to the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo: its modal UK like-for-like growth for the first half of FY2027 is 2.5% (alarm below 1.5%, upgrade trigger at 3% or more, invalidation below 0%), after about 1.5% in the second half of FY2026 (earnings review). The research filed on 3-Nov finds UK practice volumes "subdued through late 2026" and US practice visits down 2.2% in the third quarter, the eighteenth falling quarter (Morgan Stanley). Test: like-for-like growth at or above 3% at the 25-Nov annual general meeting would give analysts a reason to follow the SP; below 1.5% would leave a 19.9% rise with no estimate support.

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

The sell-side moved against the SP on 3-Nov: CVS Group's average PT fell 2.5% to 1,605p, a third analyst cut, and a third non-Buy rating appeared, on the day CVS Group closed at a nine-month high.

  • The average PT was 1,626p from 12 to 28-Oct, 1,645p on 2-Nov and 1,605p on 3-Nov (data file, daily series). PT Net Upgrade Breadth went from (18%) (none raising, 2 cutting, of 11) at 30-Oct to (27%) (none raising, 3 cutting, of 11) on 3-Nov.
  • Ratings went from 9 Buys and 2 others of 11 to 9 Buys and 3 others of 12, so coverage rose by one analyst with a non-Buy rating; no analyst rates CVS Group a Sell.
  • Who is cutting, and why: not in the sources read. The AlphaSense weekly report (4-Nov) says "No new institutional sell-side ratings or updated target prices were issued for the veterinary group this week", which the data file contradicts. The named PTs on file are Deutsche Bank's Buy and 1,700p and Jefferies' Hold and 1,400p (earnings review, 24-Sep); Shore Capital initiated at Hold in September (1,400p in the earnings review, 1,250p in the research filed on 3-Nov); the research also cites an RBC target of 1,900p. Hypothesis: the new non-Buy is a Hold with a PT near or below the SP, which would pull the average down by itself, and its arrival with two more EPS estimates fits the outer-year upgrade in item 4 (Consensus by named FY).

The gap between CVS Group's average PT and its SP is now 17.4%, narrower than at any month-end since August 2023 (14.5%). It was 22.5% at the 30-Oct month-end, 26.1% at the August month-end and about 44% at the 2-Oct low. Precedent: after the 14.5% gap of August 2023 CVS Group fell 23.1% in September 2023 and reached 974.9p by the March 2024 month-end (the cause is not in the sources read); after the 21.0% gap of May 2025 it rose on the Competition and Markets Authority's provisional decision. News, not the gap, set the direction both times. Jefferies' 1,400p is 2.4% above the 3-Nov close.

Ratings follow valuation more than momentum: CVS Group's Buys rose after falls (March 2025, July 2026) and slipped at highs (February 2026); the first rating change after a 19.9% rise is a new non-Buy, as the previous note expected.

Net Upgrade Breadth splits: analysts raise revenue and cut PTs and EPS.

  • Revenue: +33% (3 of 9 raising, none cutting), unchanged since 30-Oct and the highest month-end reading in 14 months.
  • PT: (27%), below every month-end reading since January 2025 ((44%)), including February 2025's (22%).
  • EPS (FY2027): (8%) on 3-Nov (1 raising, 2 cutting, of 12), from (40%) at 30-Sep. EBITDA (FY2027): +11% (1 of 9 raising).
  • Reading: more revenue, much of it acquired (Australia, the £15.0m two-site UK practice; earnings review), valued lower per share while the chief executive succession stays open; Sell-side Momentum is D (2-Nov).

The link to the sell tests: PT Net Upgrade Breadth below zero is already one half of the test "SS Sales or SS PT Net Upgrade Breadth below zero AND Stock RS below 50"; with breadth now (27%), only CVS Group's Stock RS (61) keeps it from a sell signal at the 30-Nov month-end.

CVS Group trades with the pet-care stocks and with UK-listed stocks, not with its hospital and clinic cohort, and on 3-Nov it outperformed both.

Hypothesis: investors are buying veterinary and pet-care exposure and UK-listed defensives as a group, and CVS Group, the laggard after its 24-Sep results, is catching up; 3-Nov is the first day it held up while both groups fell. Test: whether CVS Group holds 1,328p if Pets at Home and Vimian pull back from their highs.

Your four peer checks, for the hospital and clinic cohort and the sector:

  • Sell-side estimates: 3-month EPS revisions run from Spire Healthcare's +35% to Terveystalo's (17%), with Medicover's revenue breadth (60%); CVS Group's 0% sits between. Pets at Home's and Vimian's estimates are not in the data file.

What the peers imply for CVS Group's sell tests: the Sector RS of Hospitals, clinics and care homes (68) rests on Ambea, Medicover, Spire Healthcare and Attendo, and Attendo and Fresenius Medical Care are weakening. CVS Group is paid privately by pet owners, unlike Fresenius Medical Care's public tariffs, but the Sector RS test reads the whole sector.
