What the rules say for CVS Group now, and what decides the next step

The three findings that matter most:

  • A breakout that held its first test, on light volume: the 3-Nov dip to 1,321p held the 7-Sep high and CVS Group closed at 1,367p as the Stoxx Europe 600 fell 0.6%; distribution days fall to 2 by Fri 6-Nov, but the advance's up-day volume still trails its down-day volume (0.89).
  • Price ahead of estimates, with the sell-side leaning the other way: FY2027 EPS is (0.1%) over 3 months, which fails your "inviolate rule" of upgrades over 3 to 6 months; on 3-Nov the average PT fell 2.5% to 1,605p, 17.4% above the SP, and a new non-Buy rating appeared.
  • A sale on a lagging reading while the rest improves: the falling relative 200D MA gives a sell signal until the Fri 15-Jan-2027 reading at a flat relative price, while CVS Group's RS readings, relative price and distribution-day count move away from their lines.

The whole holding: the relative 200D MA fell 1.54% over the month to the Fri 30-Oct reading, a sell signal. Under your one-off in/out link both tranches (1.05% and 1.60% of capital now, 1.0% and 1.5% at entry) are sold when CVS Group's replacement is bought, in the APM's order of sale; the order-state text's "SELL on Tuesday 2026-11-03" is not a sale due at a session.

  • How they interact: a repeat of September's 14.2% fall at a flat market (to about 1,173p) would likely give the second sell signal through Stock RS, leave relative price about 23% below its 52W high, and add a third only if the sector's Sector RS also fell below 50, as in September.

Helios Towers has now traded below its rising absolute 200D MA on three sessions running and closed above it each time, on falling volume, two sessions before the third-quarter trading update (Thu 5-Nov) that decides which way it leaves the line.

4. Consensus by named FY

Helios Towers' EPS consensus jumped 6% to 9% for all three years on 3-Nov while revenue and EBITDA barely moved; no analyst raised anything, so the jump is a change in who is counted, not an upgrade.

  • The move on 3-Nov: FY2026 EPS $0.0892 to $0.0951 (+6.5%), FY2027 $0.1384 to $0.1472 (+6.4%), FY2028 $0.1669 to $0.1824 (+9.3%). FY2027 revenue rose 0.1% (to $1,052m) and FY2027 EBITDA 0.2% (to $598.0m); FY2028 revenue 0.5% and EBITDA 0.8%.
  • Why it is a count change: EPS, EBITDA and revenue Net Upgrade Breadth all read 0% (no raise, no cut) with seven analysts, where there were six, and the Buy count rose from six to seven. If the other six were unchanged, the seventh analyst's FY2027 figures are about $0.200 EPS, $606m EBITDA and $1,057m revenue: within 1.5% of the other six's mean on the operating lines and about 45% above it on EPS. The PT arithmetic in items 5 and 6 (Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth) names the analyst as Morgan Stanley.
  • What it means: the spread of Helios Towers' EPS estimates, the weakest line of the investment case in the memo, just widened. The new memo (written 3-Nov) holds FY2026 EPS consensus about 25% to 40% too high (its model has $0.07, against which the mean has now risen to $0.095), because first-half finance costs of $125.8m and a 45.6% interim tax rate are already in the accounts.
  • The trend before Tuesday: analysts cut EPS for all three years at the 31-Aug month-end (FY2026 by 5.7%, FY2027 by 5.1%, FY2028 by 7.7%) while holding revenue and EBITDA, and nothing moved through October. Deutsche Bank (29-Oct) gives the reason: "bottom-line projections face pressure because of non-cash foreign exchange losses and rising financing expenses".
  • Implied growth: FY2026 to FY2027, revenue +10.9%, EBITDA +12.8%, EPS +55%; FY2027 to FY2028, revenue +7.0%, EBITDA +9.0%, EPS +24%. The implied EBITDA margin rises from 55.9% to 56.9% and 57.9%: analysts model operating leverage and let only part of it reach EPS.
  • The SP leads, not the estimates: the August EPS cut came during the 20-Jul to 13-Oct rise, so October's fall is not estimate-led.
  • Test, against the memo: its re-rating branch needs brokers' EPS raised alongside EBITDA at two prints. Thursday's update carries no profit line (the memo), so EPS should not move on it; EBITDA and revenue changes after 5-Nov are the reading.

9. Peers

Helios Towers sits between two groups that are moving apart: the European towers that make up its sector are rising, and the telecom operators that are its customers and its London trading neighbours are falling.

  • SP technicals: over the month to 3-Nov Cellnex rose 15.4% and Inwit 14.1% (Inwit 9.1% above its absolute 200D MA, Cellnex 3.0% below), while Vodafone fell 7.9% (4.5% below its 50D MA) and Airtel Africa 15.1% (21.9% below its absolute 200D MA, a broken trend). Helios Towers ((5.2%)) is between them, and is the closest of the six stocks to its absolute 200D MA (1.0% above it, with its intraday lows on it; Vodafone is 1.9% above its own).
  • Sell-side estimates: Cellnex's and Inwit's 3-month revisions are flat to negative (Cellnex revenue Net Upgrade Breadth (8%)); their rally is not estimate-led.
  • Newsflow and company guidance: Richard's weekly AlphaSense report of 4-Nov carries no item on Cellnex, Inwit, Rai Way or Airtel Africa; its peers section covers other holdings' peers. The last guidance in the sources read is Airtel Africa's half-year (30-Oct): capital expenditure "more than doubled" to $657m in the half, full-year guidance of about $1.1bn reaffirmed, "a deliberate strategy to front-load deployments". That leaves about $220m a quarter for the second half ($1.1bn less $657m, over two quarters), below the memo's Alarm line of $250m and above its 1-Oct Invalidation line of $200m.
  • Hypothesis, unchanged: the European towers' October rally is a rates trade, the bond-proxy leg of the defensive rotation; Helios Towers trades instead with London-listed Africa and telecom names (its strongest own-move correlations, 0.21 to 0.24, include Vodafone). For: the new memo puts its six-month correlation with the European tower basket at 0.27. Against: the cause of the towers' 9-Oct jump is not in the sources read.

On 4-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "Telcos weak thematically (ergo Helios)" and that "the satellite disruption story and AI disruption story are a negative structural headwind".

  • For your reading: the operators' weakness is real, and they are Helios Towers' customers.
  • Against, on satellites: the new memo (3-Nov) calls satellites "a fading headwind": direct-to-cell carried 0.0003% of T-Mobile's traffic, and Airtel has signed SpaceX as a complement to its towers, not a substitute. No source read addresses an AI disruption case for towers.
  • The nearer risk the sources name is competition for the operators' capex: Esengo Towers (Vodacom and Orange, about 1,000 rural DRC sites), Eastcastle growing to 1,800 DRC sites, Indus Towers entering Africa with Airtel as anchor, and MTN taking IHS Towers in-house at about 6x EBITDA (new memo, 3-Nov). Test: any comment on competition in Thursday's update.

What the peers imply: Helios Towers' next move answers to its own 5-Nov update and to Airtel Africa's spending, not to the European towers; but its Sector RS, and so "Sector RS below 50 with Stock RS below 70", answers to the European towers, whose one-day fall on Tuesday took Sector RS from 43 to 36.

Richard decides.

IMCD's consensus has not moved for five weeks on any line or year (unchanged again on 3-Nov), and the new Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo expects the next big move in FY2027 to be down.

  • EPS over 1, 3, 6 and 12 months: FY2026 (€5.63) 0%, +1.5%, +5.7%, (6.1%); FY2027 (€6.07) 0%, +0.7%, +5.1%, (8.3%); FY2028 (€6.62) 0%, +0.9%, +5.7%, (15.4%); revenue and EBITDA follow the same shape
  • Over six months all three years rose at about the same pace, lifting the level, not the trajectory; the twelve-month cut to FY2028 ((15.4%)) has not been restored
  • Implied growth FY2026 to FY2027 is 7.7% for EPS, 5.2% for EBITDA and 4.4% for revenue (FY2027 to FY2028: 9.2%, 5.9%, 5.2%); with the EBITDA margin flat at 11.4% to 11.6%, EPS outgrowing EBITDA rests on the lines below operating profit

The memo (dated 3-Nov, published 4-Nov) puts FY2027 cash EPS at €6.15, 4% below a median consensus of €6.40, and a 60% to 65% probability that FY2027 consensus is lower by July 2027 than today. About two-thirds of its gap sits below operating profit: the refinancing of the €300m 2.125% bond due March 2027 at about 4.5%, Turkish and Argentine hyperinflation, and a 25% cash tax rate; on EBITDA the memo is within 1% of consensus. It expects FY2026 consensus to rise 1% to 2% after 5-Nov (55% probability) and FY2027 to be cut 3% to 5% over the February and July 2027 results. A caution on the figures above: three brokers on another EPS basis pull the FactSet mean (FY2027 €6.07) below the median (€6.40), so a move in the mean when they update will look like a revision without being one (memo).

The 5-Nov previews split on the line the memo's tests read. Morgan Stanley (30-Oct) forecasts third-quarter organic revenue growth of 5.0% against a company-compiled consensus of 6.1%, a gross margin of 24.9%, a conversion margin of 40.7% against the Visible Alpha consensus of 42.7%, and operating profit (EBITA) of €136m, 3% below the €140m consensus, on rising operating costs. The memo expects about €140m with organic gross profit +7% to +9%.

The link to the memo's tests: the dashboard's copy of the memo (1-Oct) moves to neutral and park on nine-month organic gross profit growth below 0%, a gross margin below 24.5%, or a second-half conversion margin below 42%. Morgan Stanley's forecast conversion margin of 40.7% would meet the conversion-margin test (below 42%); its forecast gross margin of 24.9% would not meet the gross-margin test (below 24.5%). The 3-Nov memo: "above +8% and 44% says volume; below +5% or 42% says price and costs".

On 4-Nov you wrote in your Notion note (the 2-Nov note, edited): "The SS upgrades are positive, the valuation is bottom quartile…". The upgrades are positive over six months (FY2027 EPS +5.1%) and barely positive over three (+0.7%), but have been zero for five weeks, and every Net Upgrade Breadth line is at or near zero (items 5 and 6 (Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth)). IMCD passes that rule on six months, only just on three, and the memo's base case has it failing over the next six. IMCD's PT Net Upgrade Breadth fell to zero on 3-Nov, from +7%, without a cut: the last PT raise in the 100-day window aged out. IMCD's ratings follow the gap to the PT more than the SP's momentum. Where analysts are cutting: one analyst has cut each of FY2027 EPS, EBITDA and revenue within the 100-day window; the sources read do not name the broker, and none records a PT cut since 29-Jul. On 4-Nov you wrote in your Notion note (the 2-Nov note, edited): "Buy 1.5% of IMCD with a SL on the 50D MA (less 6%, or the 100D less 0%)". The rules allow it today, and the stop-loss buffer is editable per tranche:

  • On the 50D MA less 6%: stop-loss about €92.69, 11.1% below the close; a test starts only on a close 5.5% lower, so a results-day fall of 2.0% to 5.5% would start tranche 1's test but not the new tranche's; a fall like November 2025's (7.4%) would start both, without reaching the new tranche's stop-loss
  • On the 100D MA less 0%: €94.22, 9.7% below the close; with no buffer the first close at or below the 100D MA confirms the exit that day
  • Size: a 1.5% tranche loses about 0.17% (50D) or 0.15% (100D) of capital at its stop-loss, inside the rule's 0.5% loss budget and below its 3% tranche cap; IMCD would be 3.65% of capital (2.15% now) and Industrials and capital goods about 23.4% (21.9% now), inside the 30% cap
  • For your ruling: the size, the MA and buffer, and whether to buy before or after 5-Nov; the 3-Nov memo argues for sizing "for a six-month wait rather than a six-month payoff"

You also wrote: "Then - IMCD becomes a Core Middle Innings holding if earnings are positive + well received by stock market (end of this week). Buy another 1.5% on that node". Under the rules a Probing Bet becomes a Core Investment on entering the Pool of Stocks Eligible for Middle Innings Portfolio Selection, which needs Stock RS of 80 or more on two Fridays in a row: IMCD's is 71, so the earliest entry is Fri 13-Nov. The third purchase needs that promotion, because a Probing Bet holds at most two tranches. As a Core Investment, tranche 1 (2.0% at cost) would be split into 1.67% plus a remainder, which the Position Management System offers to do, and the six Sell Criteria would sell IMCD, Industry RS (46) the nearest. For your ruling: a promotion before IMCD enters the Pool.

What would change this reading: a 5-Nov close above €104.7 on about 191,000 shares makes it the third stage, a breakout after a healthy retest of the rising 20D MA; a close at or below about €102.25 starts tranche 1's test and suspends adds.

Richard decides.

Volex's consensus did not change in the session to 3-Nov; the trajectory still rises at every horizon, and the pace has slowed to low single digits a month.

  • Volex's FY2027 (year to March 2027) EPS, $0.521: +2.9% over 1 month, +15.0% over 3 months, +20.0% over 6 months. FY2028 EPS, $0.579: +2.5%, +14.4%, +21.1%. FY2029 EPS, $0.638: +2.2%, +13.1% (first estimate 30-Apr).

  • Implied growth: Volex's FY2027 to FY2028 EPS growth is 11.1% and FY2028 to FY2029 10.3%; revenue growth is 7.2% and 6.9%. The later years rise about as fast as FY2027, so analysts are raising the trajectory, not pulling earnings forward.
  • Implied margin: Volex's FY2027 EBITDA margin is 13.6%, from 13.2% three months ago; with EPS +15.0%, EBITDA +10.5% and revenue +6.8% over 3 months, the upgrades are margin first and below the EBITDA line second.

On 3-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "We cannot own stocks that are not upgrading over N3-6M - its an inviolate rule". Volex passes it today: FY2027 EPS +15.0% over 3 months and +20.0% over 6 months, with 7 of 7 analysts raising. The risk to it is arithmetic: the 4-Nov Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo expects the 3-month figure to fall to low single digits by late January, because the window will then hold only the 12-Nov results, and puts the scope for further FY2027 upgrades at 1% to 3% and for FY2028 at nil.

Volex's SP led its estimates up to 6-Oct; since then the SP has fallen 19.0% while FY2028 EPS rose about 2.5%. Hypothesis: the market is discounting a FY2028 data-centre risk consensus does not show: a flat second half after about $10m pulled into the first half, and a largest customer (18.7% of FY2026 revenue, about the size of the data-centre line) on one-to-two-year contracts (Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo, 4-Nov). Against it: the 19-Oct update said data-centre revenue stays well ahead of the prior year. Tests at the 12-Nov results: H1 underlying operating profit at or above $74m; working capital below 28.4% of revenue; the largest customer at or below 18.7% of revenue; cash conversion back above 70%.

DNB Carnegie's Carl Korsheden reiterated Buy and trimmed his PT to SEK 340 from 342 on 26-Oct, "citing a reduction in peer group multiples" (earnings review). Against it, RedEye raised its base case to SEK 315 on 29-Oct on "a strong sales beat" (AlphaSense weekly report, 4-Nov). The Sector Digest (30-Oct) reads the pattern as "a lower multiple for Swedish serial acquirers appears to cap PTs (hypothesis)".

Ratings carry little signal; the PT does the work: every analyst rated Sdiptech a Buy through its fall to the 9-Feb low, and the average PT followed the SP, down 35% from August 2024 to January 2026 and up 19% since.

Sdiptech's PT ceiling has come down with the average. At three highs in 2026 (12-Jun, 31-Aug and 14-Oct), Sdiptech's average PT stood only about 12% to 16% above its SP, and the SP then fell 6.7% to 9.6% before analysts raised the PT. At the 14-Oct high of 270.6 the average PT of 304.8 was 12.6% above the SP. On 3-Nov the average PT of 308.1 is 17.8% above the close of 261.6. With the PT 15% above it, mid-band, the SP would be 308.1 divided by 1.15, about 268, below the 14-Oct high; a week earlier, on a PT of 312.7, the same calculation gave about 272. Test: a Sdiptech close above 268 to 270.6 without a further PT rise breaks the ceiling pattern; a stall there fits it. Test of breadth: PT or EPS Net Upgrade Breadth at or below zero at the 30-Nov month-end would be the first broker turn against the SP since January.

9. Peers

Sdiptech leads its cohort (diversified serial acquirers in a reset) on estimates and technicals; on 3-Nov the cohort split again, and Sdiptech still trades with Swedish small and mid-cap industrials rather than with its cohort.

  • Sell-side estimates: Sdiptech's 3-month EPS revision is +7% with revenue Net Upgrade Breadth +71%; Indus Holding's +5% with 0%, Storskogen's (1%) with +60%, Volati's (4%) with (100%). Lifco, the sector's largest Swedish serial acquirer, is at (4%) and (55%); Röko, the strongest, at +18% and +100%.
  • SP technicals: Indus Holding fell 1.1% on 3-Nov on 0.56x and is 5.4% below its 50D MA, its Stock RS down from 97 to 87 in a month; Storskogen rose 1.1% on 1.09x and is 2.8% above its 50D MA, Stock RS 64 from 73; Lifco is 9.8% below its 50D MA with Stock RS 13 (49 a month ago). Sdiptech is 2.9% above its 50D MA with Stock RS 86.
  • Newsflow, guidance and communications: the AlphaSense weekly report (4-Nov) has nothing on Storskogen, Indus Holding, Volati, Lifco or Röko, and no peer changed guidance in the sources read; the cohort's news is still the 23-Oct reports (Lifco (12.0%) on a customer loss; Storskogen's margin up to 9.7%).

Since its 22-Oct low Sdiptech is up 3.6%, with Storskogen (+3.3%) and Röko (+6.6%) and behind Munters (+13.9%) and Bravida (+9.9%), while Indus Holding ((2.7%)), Volati ((4.5%)) and Lifco ((12.4%)) fell. Munters, the closest daily tracker outside the cohort (a weak 0.26 correlation), rose 1.1% on 3-Nov when Sdiptech fell 0.3%. Hypothesis: Sdiptech's exposure is to its industry and a Swedish rates and multiples factor, while Lifco and Indus Holding are sold on their own news (a customer loss; the tungsten windfall's unwind, memo 31-Oct). Against: ten sessions are thin evidence.

What the peers imply for Sdiptech: the cohort is not carrying it and is not yet dragging it. Tests: Indus Holding's report on 12-Nov for the cohort's estimates (the memo calls it the "cohort read-across"); Sdiptech against Röko, Lifco and Munters in the sessions after the Riksbank's 4-Nov decision.

Hilton Food's 2-Nov closing high did not hold: on Tue 3-Nov the SP fell 1.5% to 723.0p, back inside its five-week range. Hilton Food's latest advance and reversal: from 11-Sep to 2-Nov the SP rose 10.1% to 734.0p over 36 sessions, through a range on closes of 694.9p (5-Oct) to 726.4p (21-Oct) and a +2.0% break on 2-Nov (739.0p intraday) that was mostly the cohort's (the peer cohort rose 2.5% that day); then on 3-Nov the SP fell 1.5% to 723.0p. Hilton Food's consensus has not moved since 8-Oct; the new Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo (3-Nov) judges the next move in FY2027 EPS more likely down than up. Hilton Food's financial year ends in December; figures are in sterling. No estimate changed between the 2-Nov and 3-Nov readings.

  • EPS over 1, 3 and 6 months: FY2026 52.2p, +1.5%, +6.9%, +9.7%; FY2027 56.9p, +1.0%, +6.0%, +8.1%; FY2028 62.1p, (0.7%), +2.9%, +3.9%. Over 12 months each is still down 17% to 19%, after the November 2025 profit warning.

The A&J Memo of 3-Nov, filed since the previous run, puts a dated negative on the FY2027 line.

  • The A&J Memo reads FY2027 consensus at 57.6p (its median; the dashboard reads 56.9p) and finds it above two of the three brokers it can see (54.0p, 54.9p and Deutsche Bank's 59.8p), and about 4% above its own model.
  • The A&J Memo's model has EPS of 57p, 56p and 62p for FY2026 to FY2028: FY2027 flat, against consensus's +9.0%. The A&J Memo's reasons: Canada's plant carries fixed cost before volume, the finance charge rises about £3.5m as capitalised interest ends and Bank Rate rises, and the April 2027 wage reset comes before the open-book reset recovers it. No broker models those three items, the memo finds.
  • The A&J Memo's probable sequence: a confirmation at the January 2027 trading update; on 7-Apr-2027 a small FY2026 beat on the guided 28% tax rate (consensus implies about 29.7%), and a FY2027 range at or below consensus (45% below, 40% in line, 15% above); then a 3% to 6% cut to FY2027 consensus, led by Deutsche Bank.
  • Against the memo: management's informal commentary runs above its formal numbers, and a resolution of the Foppen export restriction would add 2p to 3p to FY2027 EPS (the memo's own counter-case).

On 3-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "We cannot own stocks that are not upgrading over N3-6M - its an inviolate rule". Hilton Food passes that rule today: FY2027 EPS is up 6.0% over 3 months and 8.1% over 6 months. The memo's sequence would put it at risk from April 2027, not before; the next estimate event is the January 2027 trading update. The SP has run far ahead of the estimates: over 3 months the SP is up 18.8% and FY2027 EPS 6.0%, consistent with your 3-Nov note that "price leads estimates".

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

Hilton Food's ratings follow its SP: unanimous Buys after a 59% rise, as at the 2025 top. Hilton Food has 4 Buys of 4 on 3-Nov, unchanged since 31-Oct; it had 2 Buys and 2 other at the July and August month-ends, before the 3-Sep results, and 5 Buys and 1 other at the May 2025 month-end, the month of its 905.1p high, before a 49.6% fall. None was raised at the 29-Jan low. The A&J Memo of 3-Nov reads it as a risk: "unanimous Buys and thin liquidity".

Hilton Food's average PT (795p since 8-Oct) is 10.0% above the SP, from 8.3% on 2-Nov. The gap was 3.9% at the 30-Sep month-end, the narrowest since at least August 2024. Named PTs: Deutsche Bank 850p from 815p, Buy (3-Sep, earnings review); Berenberg 810p from 790p (7-Sep, as read for the 28-Oct note); Peel Hunt 720p, which the memo of 3-Nov notes "is at the price". The 8-Oct step in the average looks like a change in the analysts counted (5 to 4 that day), not a raise.

Net Upgrade Breadth is positive on every measure, unchanged since 31-Oct, and fading as September's raises age.

  • FY2027 EPS +40% (3 raising, 1 cutting, of 5); FY2027 EBITDA +60% (3 raising, none cutting); revenue +40% (2 raising, none cutting); PT +50% (2 raising, none cutting, of 4). At the 30-Sep month-end the same measures read +50%, +83%, +50% and +60%.
  • Where an analyst is cutting: one of Hilton Food's five analysts has cut FY2027 EPS, a cut carried since the 30-Sep month-end. The sources read do not name the broker. The stated logic in the sources for lower EPS is below EBITDA: Deutsche Bank's post-results forecasts "incorporating updated foreign exchange rates and borrowing costs" (AlphaSense research of 2-Nov, as read for the 2-Nov note), and the memo's finance-charge step-up.

Precedent: Hilton Food's EBITDA and PT breadth turned positive at the April 2026 month-end, eleven weeks before the SP crossed its absolute 200D MA on 15-Jul; now the SP leads and the breadth fades. Test: new raises after the January 2027 trading update, or the first PT cut since the March 2026 month-end.

Precedent: the last breakout attempt, 4-Sep (+3.4% to €180.4 at the high, on 1.32x, dashboard), reversed the next session and led into an 11.3% fall to 24-Sep; 3-Nov reached that level only intraday and closed in the lower half of its range.

Healthy retest or broken trend? Fevertree against your trend-health tests

  • The absolute 200D MA rising: partly; +0.9% over 20 sessions and +1.3% over 63, close to flat.
  • The 150D above the absolute 200D: fails; 1.4% below, narrowing.
  • The 50D above the 150D and the absolute 200D: passes; 862.1p against 830.5p and 842.6p.
  • The SP above each: passes; 6.8% above the 50D, 10.9% above the 150D, 9.3% above the absolute 200D.
  • Near its 52W high: passes; 5.0% below the 969.79p closing high of 13-Feb.
  • Away from its low: passes; 24.7% above the 738.45p closing low of 4-Jun.
  • Third-stage warning sign, the largest one-day or one-week fall since the advance began: partly; 3-Nov's (2.38%) edges out 18-Sep's as the largest one-day fall since 10-Sep, on ordinary volume and with no news; the largest weekly fall, (3.2%) to 29-Oct, is below 22 to 28-Sep's (5.0%).
  • Third-stage warning sign, wider swings: absent; the average daily range is 2.9% over 10 sessions, against 4.3% over the 10 before, though 3-Nov's was 4.9%.
  • Third-stage warning sign, lower lows: absent; 3-Nov's 921.0p is above 29-Oct's 911.0p, itself above 13-Oct's 868.5p.
  • Third-stage warning sign, more down days than up days: partly; 3 down and 2 up over 5 sessions, 11 up and 8 down over 20.
  • Third-stage warning sign, a light-volume breakout reversed on heavy volume: absent; 2-Nov's 943.5p was not a breakout, and 3-Nov reversed it on 1.00x.
  • Third-stage warning sign, the SP tracking a rising MA instead of bouncing off it: partly; Fevertree touched the rising 20D (907.2p) intraday on 29-Oct and 3-Nov and held it on both closes.
  • Verdict: still a healthy pullback to the rising 20D MA inside a 12-month base, with 3-Nov the first warning sign. What would settle it: a close below the 20D (907.2p, a 1.5% fall) on 1.25x the 50D average volume or more would make the pullback a distribution; a close above 969.79p on 1.5x or more would make it a breakout.

2. Volume, and relative price against its 52W high

Basis: the volume script's ratios leave out two mechanical-volume days (31-Aug and 18-Sep, which still sit in the 50D average); the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's figure is in brackets where it differs.

Fevertree's 3-Nov fall came on ordinary volume and closed mid-range: not a distribution day, but the same size and volume as the first day of four of its last five declines. On 3-Nov Fevertree traded 497,664 shares (£4.6m), 1.00x the 50D average, opened with a 4.9% gap down and closed at 53% of the day's range. Your 28-Oct definition of a distribution day needs 1.25x or more, so 3-Nov does not count. The declines from the 22-Oct-2025, 20-Apr, 17-Jun and 7-Aug highs began within two sessions with a fall of 2.1% to 3.0% on 0.44x to 1.15x, the same as 3-Nov; but each of those days closed at 0% to 33% of its range, where 3-Nov closed at 53% after recovering most of its gap. Hypothesis: 2-Nov's 943.5p is this advance's high. Test: a further fall on 4 or 5-Nov closing in the lower third of the range would match the precedent; a close back above 943.5p would refute it.

Fevertree's heavy volume all came in one burst, 6 to 15-Oct, and none has followed in the 13 sessions since. The burst (three days of 5.3x to 7.0x, £14.5m to £25.4m each) read as a large seller absorbed: 13-Oct, the day the Rolls trust crossed below 4%, closed at 10% of its range on 6.96x (6.92x) but held above 9-Oct's close. The volume script counts two distribution days (6 and 13-Oct) and four accumulation days (7, 9, 14 and 15-Oct) in 25 sessions; the dashboard counts one distribution day, unchanged from 27-Oct.

Volume has dried up through the pullback, on both sides. The six down days since 22-Oct ran 0.76x to 1.12x (average 0.92x) and the two up days 0.76x and 0.92x. The dashboard's up/down volume ratio is 0.95 over 5 sessions and 0.91 over 20, down from 0.99 over 20 sessions on 2-Nov and 1.16 on 27-Oct; its 20D average volume is 0.83x its 50D average. The advance from the 10-Sep low (+19.9% over 38 sessions) runs at 1.17 average up-day against down-day volume, inside the 0.88 to 1.39 of the four earlier advances of the last 12 months, each of which gave way to a fall of 9.6% to 22.7%.

Precedent: Fevertree's lows have been marked by volume; its tops have not. Four of the last five lows were followed within one to four sessions by an up day on 1.9x to 2.5x (24-Mar, 9-Jun, 14-Jul, 11-Sep). In the five declines since October 2025 the first close at or below the 50D came 0.8% to 8.4% below the high, every time on 0.35x to 0.95x; the 50D now stands 8.6% below the 2-Nov high.

Fevertree's relative price has not confirmed the SP since 23-Oct. Relative price is 3.6% below its 23-Oct peak (526.8), while the SP made a higher close on 2-Nov, and 12.8% below its 52W high (13-Feb), against 5.0% for the SP. Relative price has stayed above its relative 200D MA since 12-Oct, 4.4% above now against 6.6% on 2-Nov; the previous cross above, on 11-Feb, failed by 3-Mar before the 22.7% fall to 20-Mar.

Peers' volume on 3-Nov puts the heavier selling at Remy (1.76x, (3.3%)), AB InBev (1.35x, (1.4%)) and Coca-Cola Europacific Partners (1.27x, (3.5%), its third-quarter day), not at Fevertree.

Confirms the advance: a close above 969.79p on 1.5x the 50D average volume or more, in the top third of the range, with relative price above its 23-Oct peak. Confirms a turn: a close below the 20D MA (907.2p) on 1.25x or more, then a close at or below the 50D MA (862.1p, a 6.4% fall), which starts tranche 1's test.

3. RS

Fevertree's Stock RS lost 6 points in one session, to 60, and Beverages' Sector RS lost 7, to 74; Consumer staples' Industry RS held at 66. The fall came at the sector level, not the industry level.

  • Fevertree's Stock RS is 60 on 3-Nov: 66 on 2-Nov, 61 at the 30-Oct month-end, 54 on 2-Oct (1M), 30 in early August (3M), 33 a year ago (12M). It peaked at 68 on 23-Oct and is 10 points above the 50 line in the Net Upgrade Breadth Sell Criterion and 10 below the 70 line in the Sector RS Sell Criterion.
  • Beverages' Sector RS is 74: 81 on 2-Nov, 76 at the 30-Oct month-end, 50 on 2-Oct, 23 a year ago; 85 at 23-Oct was its 13-month high. It is 24 points above the 50 line.
  • Consumer staples' Industry RS is 66, unchanged since the 30-Oct month-end: 33 on 2-Oct, 20 a year ago, 73 at 23-Oct. It is 36 points above the 30 line.

The turn went Fevertree first (from 11-Sep), then the sector (25-Sep), then the industry (9-Oct); the latest dip starts in the sector. On 3-Nov the spirits-linked and soft-drinks names lost RS (Diageo 61 to 53, Coca-Cola Europacific Partners 82 to 75, Coca-Cola HBC 70 to 64, Remy 82 to 77) while the brewers held (Heineken 69 to 70, Carlsberg 77 to 76, AB InBev 70 to 68).

Precedent: the last time Fevertree's Stock RS stood in the 60s (65 at the 27-Feb month-end, near the 52W high), it fell to 27 by the 31-Mar month-end as the SP fell 22.7%. Test: the Industry Digest's test of a durable rotation rather than de-grossing is Consumer staples' Industry RS above 50 on both 30-Oct (passed, 66) and the Friday 6-Nov reading.

4. Consensus by named FY

Fevertree's consensus is unchanged in the session and has edged down over the periods your upgrade rule reads: no FY2026 to FY2028 line has risen over 3 months, and FY2027 EPS is flat.

  • EPS: FY2026 28.0p, (0.4%) over 1M, flat over 3M, (2.0%) over 6M; FY2027 38.3p, (0.5%), flat, +0.2% over 6M and +2.1% over 12M; FY2028 48.6p, (0.3%), (1.0%), +1.7% over 6M.
  • Revenue: FY2026 £400.6m, (0.1%) over 1M, (0.5%) over 3M; FY2027 £433.0m, (0.3%), (1.2%), and (3.1%) over 12M; FY2028 £464.6m, (0.4%), (2.6%), and (5.0%) over 12M.
  • EBITDA: FY2026 £49.8m, flat over 1M, (0.7%) over 3M; FY2027 £63.9m, (0.3%), (1.6%), (1.9%) over 6M; FY2028 £77.6m, (0.4%), (4.0%), and (4.9%) over 12M.

On 3-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "We cannot own stocks that are not upgrading over N3-6M - its an inviolate rule". Your rule reads forward, over the next 3 to 6 months, so it cannot yet be failed; but on the last 3 and 6 months Fevertree is not upgrading, and the new investment memo expects it not to over the next 6 to 12. The out-years are cut fastest (FY2028 EBITDA (4.0%) over 3M against FY2026's (0.7%)), so analysts are trimming the trajectory the case depends on, while the implied EBITDA margin still climbs from 12.4% (FY2026) to 14.7% and 16.7%. The investment memo written on 3-Nov (filed 4-Nov) puts its own base 15% below consensus on FY2027 EPS and 20% below on FY2028, expects "a 5-9% trim to FY2027-28 EBITDA consensus", and gives a 65% chance that FY2027 EBITDA consensus falls more than 3% within twelve months. Test: FY2027 EBITDA consensus after the January 2027 pre-close.

The investment memo's main new finding changes what your Nielsen check can tell you. It judges the guaranteed minimum United States royalty "probably binding now": Rothschild & Co Redburn's estimate of the joint operation's 2026 margin (5.4% on about £143m) implies a Fevertree half of £3.5m to £4m, while Fevertree booked £4.1m for the first half alone. While the floor binds (FY2026 and probably FY2027), a strong United States scanner print moves the narrative and the multiple, not EPS.

On 2-Nov you wrote: "I must get the Nielsen check and see what it says". The key-question research filed on 3-Nov answers the European half; the United States half is still July and August's 16%. Morgan Stanley (30-Oct) put Fevertree's European sales up 6.6% in the 12 weeks to 4-Oct and its European volume share up 10 basis points, but its value share down 3 basis points in Germany and 1 in Spain over four weeks; Jefferies (30-Oct) had European soft drinks value up 7.8% in the four weeks, and Fevertree's "domestic" retail sales growth slowing to 5.4% from 10.4% (the research does not name the market). Rothschild & Co Redburn (2-Nov) says premium mixers lag wider soft drinks in Europe. The European read is mildly negative and small against the United States question.

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

Fevertree's SP turned down from its 2-Nov close of 943.5p, 0.4% short of its average PT (947.3p), and the 3-Nov close of 921.0p is 2.8% below it; the median PT, about 875p (investment memo, 3-Nov), is 5.0% below the SP. The two latest times the SP reached the average PT, at the July 2025 and February 2026 month-ends, were followed by falls of 14.9% and 20.0% by the next month-end. Hypothesis: the flat PT acts as a ceiling, as it did then. Test: a close above 947.3p with PTs rising would break the pattern.

Ratings, PT and breadth are unchanged in the session, and the AlphaSense report records "no material rating changes" in the last week of October. Fevertree has 7 Buys, 5 other and 3 Sells (15 analysts), unchanged since the 30-Sep month-end. Its ratings have followed falls, not rises (Buys to 5 after the 18% fall to May, to 7 after the results low), so new Buys are unlikely near the average PT.

Fevertree's Net Upgrade Breadth is split:

  • EPS (FY2027): 0% (6 raising, 6 cutting, of 15), down from +50% at July and +7% at September.
  • EBITDA (FY2027): +36% (7 raising, 2 cutting, of 14), from (36%) at August, yet FY2027 EBITDA consensus fell 0.3% over the month: small raises against larger cuts.
  • Revenue (FY2027): (9%) (2 raising, 3 cutting, of 11), negative at every month-end since August 2025 except July 2026.
  • PT: (13%) (3 raising, 5 cutting, of 15), from (7%) at the 30-Sep month-end.

On 2-Nov you wrote: "The problem is the SS momentum is negative… Major problem". The readings agree: the Eight Master Ratings put Sell-side Momentum at D (2-Nov), and revenue and PT breadth below zero are one half of the Net Upgrade Breadth Sell Criterion (item 11 (the Sell Criteria)). The brokers cutting are still not named in the sources read. The nearest stated logic is Rothschild & Co Redburn's (Sell), which calls the United States consensus "lofty", and Deutsche Bank's warning that growth may need permanently elevated marketing (investment memo, 3-Nov); against them, Redburn raised its FY2027 and FY2028 EPS 4% to 6% in September on "better-modelled guaranteed EBITDA". What would resolve it: the five PT-cutting brokers' notes, which AlphaSense did not surface this week.

9. Peers

Fevertree trades with Beverages, and on 3-Nov with its spirits-linked and soft-drinks names rather than the brewers; its own part was the September rebound.

  • SP technicals: on 3-Nov Fevertree's (2.4%) matched Diageo (2.4%) and sat beside Remy (3.3%) and Coca-Cola Europacific Partners (3.5%), while the brewers held (Carlsberg (0.5%), Heineken (0.8%), Royal Unibrew +0.6%), as did Campari (0.5%); Consumer staples fell 0.5%. From the 29-Oct close to 3-Nov Fevertree's +1.1% was the second-smallest rise in the sector table, against Carlsberg +7.1%, Royal Unibrew +5.6% and Pernod Ricard +4.5%. Only from the 10-Sep low does Fevertree lead (+19.9%; Remy +17.6%).
  • Sell-side estimates: revenue Net Upgrade Breadth is strongly positive at Remy (+53%), Carlsberg (+52%), Campari (+43%), Coca-Cola HBC (+22%) and Heineken (+15%), and Coca-Cola HBC's 3M revenue revision is +30%; Fevertree's revenue breadth of (9%) sits with Diageo (16%) and AB InBev (20%), the negative names.
  • Newsflow: Heineken's third-quarter organic volume grew 3.9% against 1.8% expected, with guidance kept (Bernstein, 29-Oct); Carlsberg's soft drinks grew 9% in its third quarter, with guidance of 4% to 6% kept (AlphaSense report, 4-Nov); Coca-Cola Europacific Partners' third-quarter FX-neutral sales grew 5.8% against 5.0% expected, with guidance reiterated (Deutsche Bank, 3-Nov), yet its shares fell 3.5% that day.
  • Company guidance and communications: no peer guidance in the sources read touches mixers. Fevertree's United States partner, Molson Coors, reports its third quarter on Thursday 5-Nov; the investment memo (3-Nov) names that call (2027 incentives on Fevertree volume, marketing, cans onshoring) as the next dated test of the case.

On 2-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "Decent chance the SP is carried up on sector sentiment only". The evidence since 22-Oct supports you; the evidence from September does not. Since 22-Oct Fevertree has fallen and risen with Beverages, and on 3-Nov it lost RS with Diageo and the Coca-Cola bottlers (item 3 (RS)). Fevertree's own part was the 10 to 22-Sep rebound and the absorption of the Rolls trust's sale (items 1 and 2), neither backed by estimates.

Which group Fevertree trades with now: outside its cohort, AB InBev, Carlsberg, Heineken and Pernod Ricard track its daily moves most closely (average correlation 0.35 to 0.40, moderate); the investment memo (3-Nov) finds the beverage basket explains only 21% of Fevertree's daily variance.

What the peers imply: soft drinks were the strong line in the peers' third quarters, which supports the brewers'-network part of the case, yet the market sold the soft-drinks and spirits names on 3-Nov. A repeat of the 22 to 29-Oct sector fall (Fevertree (3.2%)) would take Fevertree below its 20D MA, not to its 50D. Test: whether Fevertree holds its 20D MA on days Beverages falls, over the two weeks to 13-Nov, and Molson Coors' 5-Nov call.

What the rules say for Fevertree now, and what decides the next step

The findings that matter most:

  • The sell-side is not upgrading, so your 3-Nov rule (upgrades over the next 3 to 6 months) is not yet met, and the record and the memo point against it: FY2027 EPS is flat over 3M, FY2027 revenue (1.2%) and EBITDA (1.6%), FY2028 EBITDA (4.0%), with revenue and PT breadth negative; the 3-Nov investment memo expects trims, not upgrades, over the next 6 to 12 months (items 4, 5 and 6).
  • The SP is the sector's, and 3-Nov was the first warning: the largest one-day fall since the 10-Sep advance began, a 4.9% opening gap with no cause in the sources read, and a 6-point drop in Stock RS, while relative price has not confirmed the SP since 23-Oct (items 1, 2 and 3).
  • Your Nielsen condition is still unmet: the European scanner read to 4-Oct is mildly negative, no United States print since July and August's 16% is in the sources read, and while the royalty floor binds a strong print would lift the multiple, not EPS (item 4 (Consensus by named FY)).

Tranche 1 (2.43% of capital now, 2.0% at entry; 50D MA) is clear under the 10-day stop-loss rule. Fevertree closed at 921.0p, 6.8% above the 50D MA (862.1p) and 13.6% above the stop-loss (810.4p, the MA less 6%); a 6.4% fall starts a test and a 12.0% fall reaches the stop-loss. Its exit confirmed on 3-Sep-2026 was cleared under your 2-Nov-2026 ruling (the rule applies from 2-Nov; earlier confirmations whose price had closed back above the MA were cleared), and the tranche is judged afresh from 2-Nov.

The nearest sell test is tranche 1's 50D MA, and it is coming closer from both sides.

  • Pace: Fevertree's close stood 6.8% above the 50D on 3-Nov, against 8.2% a week earlier, 1.3% below it four weeks earlier and 5.7% above it 13 weeks earlier. At a flat SP the rising 50D reaches 866.3p by 6-Nov and 875.3p by 13-Nov, so the fall that starts a test shrinks from 6.4% to 5.0%.
  • What the rule then does: on Fevertree the volume test decides fast. Its down days since 22-Oct have run 0.76x to 1.12x the 50D average, so a second close at or below the 50D on ordinary volume would very likely confirm the exit on day 2 (the days-after volume average reaching 0.75 times the 50-day average), and tranche 1 would sell at the following Tuesday session. A close at or below the stop-loss confirms it on day 1.
  • The record: each of Fevertree's five declines since October 2025 went on, after its first close at or below the 50D, to a low of 738p to 769p; after the 7-Aug high the exit came on 3-Sep at 814.9p, 6.0% above the 768.45p low of 10-Sep. The counter-case: closes below the 50D on 1 and 2-Oct would have confirmed an exit on 2-Oct, before the 13.2% rise to 943.5p.

The next reading day, Fri 6-Nov-2026, reads Consumer staples' Industry RS (66, 36 points above the 30 line) and the relative 200D MA flat or falling over the month (falling 0.29% today; at a flat relative price it reads (0.26%) on 6-Nov, and clearing it needs a close of about 1,075p). Neither sells Fevertree: both are information for a Probing Bet. Molson Coors' third-quarter call on Thursday 5-Nov is the first dated fundamental test.

The rules allow an add: tranche 1 is clear, and Fevertree holds 1 of 2 tranches and is 2.43% of capital now against its 6% limit, leaving 3.57% of room; the next tranche sizes at 3% of capital, above the 1-2% your 28-Oct note named.

Where Fevertree stands for an add: the second stage, may be retesting a rising MA. The Master Dashboard's tests point the same way (pulling back within its medium- and long-term uptrend, probable, 4 of 4 tests; a healthy retest plausible, 4 of 6; a volatility contraction pattern plausible, 3 of 4). The SP has twice held the rising 20D intraday, but 3-Nov's fall stops it short of the third stage. Your add trigger, a positive retest of a rising MA, is partly present; the broadening of the case is absent.

  • For your ruling: your 2-Nov note calls Fevertree a "Questionable add candidate" and says positions are "too small ATM"; your 3-Nov rule that "we cannot own stocks that are not upgrading over N3-6M" points the other way, and the Position Entry and Exit Rules do not yet contain it.

Dates: Molson Coors (5-Nov); the 6-Nov reading; the September and October United States scanner data (the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo's Alarm: below 10% for a quarter); the January 2027 pre-close; full-year results, projected for 30-Mar-2027. What would change this reading: a close above 969.79p on 1.5x volume or more (the third stage, a breakout); a close below the 20D (907.2p) on 1.25x or more (back to the first stage); a close at or below the 50D (a test, and adds suspended).

Richard decides.

    • Why: It passes the other two: market capitalisation about €1,034m against €500m, and Beverages' Sector RS 74 against 50.
    • What would change it: Stock RS up 20 points and revenue breadth at zero or above, on two Fridays in a row; your 2-Nov note: "still very much a probing bet".

Carlsberg

Middle Innings Core Investment, held under Richard's one-off in/out link · 4.52% of capital in 3 tranches (tranche 1, 2.24%, dated 22-Jun-2026, the start of the position record, which may not be its purchase date; tranche 2, 0.91%, bought 7-Aug-2026; tranche 3, 1.37%, bought 19-Aug-2026) · readings as at Tue 3-Nov-2026

1. SP and MAs

Carlsberg held its breakout on 3-Nov: its SP eased 0.5% to DKK 1,000.5, 0.5% below the 52W closing high of 2-Nov (DKK 1,005.5) and 1.9% above the DKK 981.8 ceiling of 12-Feb, and the fall was the market's, not Carlsberg's. The Stoxx Europe 600 fell 0.6% and Consumer staples 0.5%; at Carlsberg's beta of 0.86 to the Stoxx Europe 600, a 0.6% market fall explains a 0.5% fall in Carlsberg in full. Carlsberg is up 32.2% over 12 months and 4.6% over 3 months; over 20 sessions it is up 16.1%, of which 14.9 points is its own move, 2.3 times its usual 20-session own move of 6.4%.

Carlsberg's legs, against the cohort brewers (AB InBev, Heineken, Royal Unibrew) and Consumer staples:

  • 18-Sep to 22-Oct, the advance: DKK 840.6 to 980.6, +16.7%; mostly the group's rotation into defensives (Consumer staples +8.2% from 1 to 22-Oct; Sector Digest, 28-Oct).
  • 22 to 29-Oct, the pullback: to DKK 934.2, (4.7%); partly the group's (Consumer staples (1.2%)) and partly Carlsberg's own, in the heavy 27-Oct selling and the 3.0% fall on the statement day, 29-Oct.
  • 29-Oct to 2-Nov, the breakout: +7.6% in two sessions against +4.7% to +5.0% for the other brewers and about +2.2% for Consumer staples: about 3 points Carlsberg's own. 3-Nov's (0.5%) was the group's (item 9 (Peers)).

Developments (AlphaSense weekly report of 4-Nov, and the earnings review):

  • 28 and 29-Oct, the third-quarter statement: organic revenue +3.0% against 2.6% consensus, organic volume +0.3% against 0.6%, China volume (10%) on weather and weak sentiment (Morgan Stanley and Deutsche Bank, 29-Oct); soft drinks +9%, Pepsi +18% (press release, 29-Oct), premium Carlsberg outside China +20% (INTRM, 29-Oct); FY2026 organic operating profit guidance of 4% to 6% reiterated. The SP fell 3.0%, closing on its low.
  • 30-Oct, management's London sell-side group meeting (Bernstein, 30-Oct): with China flat instead of (10%), revenue would have been in the target range, and pricing can "fully offset" expected material Western European cost inflation in 2027. That day Carlsberg rose 3.9% on 2.43x its 50D average volume: the reassurance, more than the statement, is the event best placed to explain the reversal.
  • 29-Oct and 3-Nov, the Pepsi build-out: Pepsi licences in Azerbaijan from January 2027 and long-term licences in 17 markets by January 2029 (INTRM, 29-Oct); all regional Pepsi volume in-house in Kazakhstan by end-2026, which management expects to lift FY2027 profitability (CIRC, 3-Nov). The 3-Nov item moved nothing visible.

Hypotheses for the breakout, ranked by evidence. 1. The statement removed an overhang and management's 2027 cost message turned the sell-side. For: the selling of 27 and 29-Oct was reversed on the meeting day on heavier volume, and Deutsche Bank and Bernstein raised PTs (items 5 and 6 (Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth)). Against: estimates rose under 1%, on currency (item 4 (Consensus by named FY)). 2. The defensive rotation resumed. For: Consumer staples rose 1.0% and 1.2% on 30-Oct and 2-Nov. Against: Carlsberg beat the brewers by about 1.5 points on each day. 3. The market began to price the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo's buyback: least supported; none was announced, and Jefferies sees "accelerated shareholder returns by mid-2027" (29-Oct). Test: Carlsberg holding DKK 981.8 while staples rest favours the first; a fall back with the group favours the second.

On 2-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "SP has popped hard like L2W in Feb-26 and Jul-26 (both times after earnings…?) - chances are it retraces somewhat here". February matches; July does not.

  • February: Carlsberg rose 19.2% in the 10 sessions to its 12-Feb high (29-Jan to 12-Feb), 10.0% of it after the close of the FY2025 results day, 4-Feb (+4.5% on 2.0x), 19.0% above its 50D MA, then fell 20.0% to 24-Mar, closing below its 50D MA 17 sessions after the high and its absolute 200D MA after 25.
  • July: the 30-Jul high followed a rise of only 3.2% in 10 sessions and came before the 19-Aug half-year results; the 12.9% fall to 18-Sep broke the 50D MA on the results day (on 1.73x).
  • Now: +6.0% over 10 sessions and +17.5% over 20 to the 2-Nov high, 12.7% above the 50D MA, less stretched than February. In neither precedent did a retest of the 50D, 100D or 150D MA hold; both lows came 2.7% to 3.3% below the absolute 200D MA.

And: "Continue to see higher highs over L6-12M". Higher lows, yes; higher highs only since 2-Nov. The swing lows rose from DKK 785.0 (24-Mar) to 817.0 (4-Jun) and 840.6 (18-Sep); the highs of 30-Jul (DKK 964.8) and 22-Oct (DKK 980.6) stayed below February's until 2-Nov.

The MAs: on 3-Nov the 150D MA rose above the absolute 200D MA, by DKK 0.41, for the first time since 9-Oct (it slipped below on 12-Oct); the order is still not an advance's. The order from highest to lowest: 20D (DKK 940.14), 100D (902.22), 50D (894.39), 150D (882.15), absolute 200D (881.74). Over 20 sessions the 20D MA rose 9.8%, the 100D 2.0%, the absolute 200D 1.5%, the 150D 1.3% and the 50D only 0.8%.

Healthy retest or broken trend? Carlsberg against your trend-health tests

  • The absolute 200D MA rising: passes; +1.5% over 20 sessions.
  • The 150D MA above the absolute 200D MA: passes, barely, from 3-Nov (DKK 882.15 against DKK 881.74).
  • The 50D MA above the 150D and absolute 200D MAs: passes, by 1.4%.
  • The SP above each MA: passes; 6.4% above the 20D MA, 13.5% above the absolute 200D MA.
  • Near its 52W high and away from its low: passes; 0.5% below the high, 36.6% above the DKK 732.64 low of 18-Nov-2025.
  • Stage 3 flags present: the largest fall since the advance began (29-Oct's (3.0%); 22 to 29-Oct's (4.7%)), on mixed news, and wider swings (an average daily move of 2.4% over 5 sessions against 1.0% over the 20 before).
  • Stage 3 flags absent: lower lows; more down days than up (20 up, 12 down since 18-Sep); a light-volume breakout reversed on heavy volume (3-Nov fell on 0.95x); the SP tracking a rising MA.
  • Verdict: a healthy advance at a new high, with two Stage 3 flags that both came from the statement. What would settle it: the volume tests at the end of item 2 (Volume, and relative price against its 52W high).

2. Volume, and relative price against its 52W high

Carlsberg's first session after its breakout was a light-volume pause, the healthy form; the breakout itself still rests on one heavy reversal day, not on heavy buying at the new high. On 3-Nov Carlsberg fell 0.5% on 0.95x its 50D average volume (225,473 shares, DKK 225.6m), in line with Consumer staples ((0.5%)) and the Stoxx Europe 600 ((0.6%)). It opened at DKK 1,006.0, traded to DKK 1,006.5, a new intraday high, then faded to close at 0.55 of the day's range: sellers did not press, buyers did not chase.

Carlsberg's heavy-volume days in the last 25 sessions (1.5x the 50D average or more, dashboard basis, the figure without the mechanical-volume days of 31-Aug and 18-Sep in brackets):

  • 15-Oct, accumulation: +3.6% on 1.48x (1.54x), 321,386 shares, DKK 299.9m, closing at 0.89 of the range after a 1.6% gap up; mostly the group's (brewers +2.6%).
  • 27-Oct, distribution: (1.4%) on 2.07x (2.14x), 466,730 shares, DKK 445.1m, closing at 0.11 of the range; Royal Unibrew fell 0.3% on 0.8x and Heineken 0.6% on 1.6x, so the selling was mostly Carlsberg's own, the session before its 28-Oct third-quarter statement. No source read names the seller.
  • 30-Oct, accumulation: +3.9% on 2.43x (2.51x), 567,548 shares, DKK 551.1m, after a 2.2% gap up; Carlsberg traded to DKK 982.0, above the 12-Feb ceiling intraday, and closed at 0.59 of the range. It was Carlsberg's heaviest up-day volume since 25-Nov-2024, on the day of management's London meeting (item 1 (SP and MAs)).
  • Below the line: 29-Oct (3.0%) on 1.30x, a 2.0% gap down and a close on the low; 2-Nov +3.6% on 1.32x (311,868 shares, DKK 313.6m), a close on the high.

Distribution against accumulation, 25 sessions: the volume script flags 2 distribution days (27 and 29-Oct, both around the statement) against 6 accumulation days (7, 14, 15, 28 and 30-Oct and 2-Nov); the dashboard counts 1 distribution day, from 2 at 6-Oct and 7 at mid-September.

Up-day against down-day volume: the ratio of volume on up days to volume on down days is 1.70 over 5 sessions (1.32 on 2-Nov) and 1.08 over 20 sessions (dashboard). Over the whole advance from 18-Sep (+19.0%, 32 sessions) it is 1.06, with 2 heavy up days and 1 heavy down day. That is the weak point: Carlsberg's September 2025 to February 2026 advance ran at 1.26 with 9 heavy up days and its March to May 2026 advance at 1.16 with 3; only the June to July 2026 advance (1.02, 1 heavy up day), which gave way to the 12.9% fall, was thinner. The volume trend (20D against 50D average) is 1.25x and rising: participation grew late, around the statement, rather than leading the advance.

Precedent: the session after the high. February's top, the closest match to now (item 1 (SP and MAs)), was followed by a 1.8% fall on 1.67x, closing at 0.26 of its range; 3-Nov fell 0.5% on 0.95x at 0.55 of its range. On the first session, this high does not look like February's. Of Carlsberg's last three lows, 24-Mar turned on heavy up days (31-Mar on 1.9x, 8-Apr on 1.5x), while 4-Jun (1.2x at most) and 18-Sep (1.3x at most) turned quietly.

Carlsberg's relative price has not confirmed the SP's new high, but it is making higher highs within the year. Relative price against the Stoxx Europe 600 is 6.4% below its 52W high of 12-Feb (6.3% on 2-Nov), while the SP is 0.5% below its own 52W high of 2-Nov. Relative price on 2-Nov was its highest since early March 2026 and above its July relative high (7-Jul). It stands 8.2% above its relative 200D MA, which it crossed on 15-Oct.

On 2-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "Good to see the relative 200D MA is positive now". Confirmed and steepening: the relative 200D MA rose 0.57% over the month to 3-Nov, from 0.49% on 2-Nov, +0.42% at the 30-Oct reading and +0.12% at 23-Oct; it was flat or falling at the Friday readings from 11-Sep to 16-Oct.

Peers' volume on 3-Nov: AB InBev fell 1.4% on 1.35x its 50D average volume, the heaviest among the brewers; Carlsberg's fall on 0.95x was the steadier tape.

Confirms the breakout: a pullback towards DKK 981.8 (1.9% below the 3-Nov close) on volume below the 50D average that holds and closes in the upper half of its range, or a close above DKK 1,006.5 on 1.5x or more. Confirms a failure: a close back below DKK 981.8 on above-average volume, then below the 29-Oct low of DKK 934.2 (6.6% below).

3. RS

Carlsberg's Stock RS held at 76 on 3-Nov, one point off its series high, while Beverages' Sector RS fell 7 points in one session: the sector's RS swings fast, and Carlsberg's own RS is the steadier of the two. Each RS is a percentile rank from 0 to 99.

  • Trend: Carlsberg's Stock RS was 48 at the Fri 2-Oct reading, 73 at Fri 23-Oct, 59 on 29-Oct (the statement day), 70 at the Fri 30-Oct month-end, 77 on 2-Nov and 76 on 3-Nov. It was 68 at the 31-Jul month-end and 22 at the 31-Oct-2025 month-end.
  • Beverages' Sector RS is 74, from 81 on 2-Nov, 76 at 30-Oct, 85 at 23-Oct and 50 at 2-Oct. It fell on a day when Diageo fell 2.4% and AB InBev 1.4%.
  • Consumer staples' Industry RS is 66, unchanged since 30-Oct (73 at 23-Oct, 59 on 29-Oct). The Industry Digest's test of a durable rotation (Industry RS above 50 at the 30-Oct and 6-Nov Fridays) passed its first reading.
  • The order of the turn: Carlsberg's Stock RS and Beverages' Sector RS turned together at the Fri 25-Sep reading; Consumer staples' Industry RS followed from 9-Oct. Beverages led from 16 to 23-Oct; since the 29-Oct statement Carlsberg has led its group. Carlsberg's Stock RS is above Beverages' Sector RS again (76 against 74).

On 2-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "Very solid looking Stock, Sector and Industry RS of 70-80 now". Mostly confirmed: Carlsberg 76 and Beverages 74 are in that band; Consumer staples' Industry RS, at 66, is below it.

Precedent: Carlsberg's Stock RS has twice topped in the high 60s and 70s, then fallen to the low 40s. It was 74 at the Fri 13-Feb reading and 42 by 20-Mar, after a 20.0% SP fall; it was 68 at the 31-Jul month-end and 41 by 18-Sep, after a 12.9% fall. A Stock RS of 76 to 77 is a new high for the series, which weakens the reading that the 70s are the top of Carlsberg's range. Refuted if Carlsberg's Stock RS holds 70 or more at the Fri 6-Nov and Fri 13-Nov readings with the SP above DKK 981.8.

Peers' Stock RS: AB InBev 68 (58 a month ago), Heineken 70 (41), Royal Unibrew 27 (11). Carlsberg leads its cohort on Stock RS.

4. Consensus by named FY

Carlsberg's consensus is rising at the margin, later years slightly faster than nearer ones, but the rise is still mostly translation currency and too small to undo the year's cuts.

  • The chart's 1, 3 and 6-month changes to 3-Nov: FY2026 EPS DKK 65.17, +0.3%, +0.6% and +0.9%; FY2027 EPS DKK 70.33, +0.7%, (0.3%) and (0.7%); FY2028 EPS DKK 76.70, +0.8%, +0.5% and 0.0%. FY2027 revenue +1.0%, +0.6% and +0.7%; FY2027 EBITDA +0.6%, (1.3%) and (1.4%).
  • Since the previous run (one session): FY2027 EPS +0.3% (DKK 70.12 to 70.33), revenue +0.2%, EBITDA +0.2%, the last post-statement updates arriving.
  • The trajectory: over the month FY2028 EPS rose 0.8% against 0.3% for FY2026, so analysts are lifting the later years a little more: a slightly raised trajectory, not earnings pulled forward. Implied EPS growth is 7.9% from FY2026 to FY2027 and 9.1% from FY2027 to FY2028, on revenue growth of 4.6% and 3.6% in those two years and an implied EBITDA margin rising from 21.0% in FY2026 to 21.3% in FY2027 and 21.7% in FY2028: a synergy and insourcing case more than a volume case.
  • Why they rose: currency, as Bernstein, Deutsche Bank and Rothschild & Co Redburn state (29 and 30-Oct; earnings review); Carlsberg raised its FY2026 translation tailwind to DKK 100m (statement of 28-Oct).

On 2-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "L1M EPS, EBITDA and Sales upgrades for consensus and same for average PT - albeit after a negative/invalidating L3M trend". Confirmed. Over one month FY2027 EPS rose 0.7%, revenue 1.0%, EBITDA 0.6% and the average PT 2.0% (DKK 1,044 at 24-Oct to DKK 1,065); over three months FY2027 EPS is (0.3%) and FY2027 EBITDA (1.3%).

On 3-Nov you wrote in your market note: "We cannot own stocks that are not upgrading over N3-6M - its an inviolate rule". Carlsberg has not yet passed it on its record: FY2027 EPS is down 0.3% over three months and 0.7% over six, and EBITDA is down for all three years over both periods; only the last month is up, on currency. The test is forward-looking: the first evidence will be whether FY2027 and FY2028 EPS keep rising once the currency changes are in, through the FY2026 results on 9-Feb-2027.

The link: the SP led and the estimates followed only a little, as your note of 3-Nov says is usual when a sector is low in range ("Almost always, price leads estimates (when a sector is low in range)"). Carlsberg's SP rose 16.1% over 20 sessions while FY2027 EPS rose 0.7% over the month. The Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo's FY2027 EPS test (modal DKK 69 to 71, alarm below DKK 66) is met at DKK 70.33. Confirms the price lead: organic, not currency, EPS raises after the next sell-side round; refutes it: FY2027 EPS back below DKK 70 with the SP holding up, an SP running on rotation alone.

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

Carlsberg's average PT has risen 2.0% since the 28-Oct reading, from DKK 1,044 to DKK 1,065 on 3-Nov, 6.4% above the SP, as the post-statement raises reached the data file; the gap has reopened. The 3.8% gap the previous run gave for 2-Nov set the 2-Nov SP against the 28-Oct average PT; the data file now carries DKK 1,062 for 2-Nov, a gap of 5.6%, so most of the rise predates 3-Nov. The average PT had been flat for a month (DKK 1,046 at 1-Oct, 1,044 at 24-Oct) while the SP rose 15.0% (1 to 23-Oct). The hypothesis of the previous note, that the average PT caps Carlsberg's SP unless PTs rise, is weakened: PTs are now rising. At the month-ends when the average PT stood 5.8% to 10.7% above the SP (May 2025, February and July 2026), the SP then fell 12% to 21%, so a gap of this size has not on its own been support.

The latest PT changes (AlphaSense weekly report of 4-Nov, and the earnings review):

  • Raised: Deutsche Bank, Buy, DKK 1,160 to DKK 1,200 (29-Oct), calling the 3% post-statement fall "a buying opportunity" on Carlsberg's multi-beverage insulation from structural alcohol headwinds; Bernstein, Outperform, DKK 1,535 to DKK 1,550 (30-Oct), with a 13% EPS compound growth rate over the four years to FY2029.
  • Held: Morgan Stanley, Overweight, DKK 1,075, Rothschild & Co Redburn, Buy, DKK 1,100, and Jefferies, Hold, DKK 950 (all 29-Oct); DNB Carnegie, Buy, DKK 995 (15-Oct).
  • Below the SP: Barclays DKK 930 (Equal Weight, 12-Oct), Jefferies DKK 950 and HSBC DKK 980 (Hold); Carlsberg's SP of DKK 1,000.5 is 2.1% to 7.6% above them.

Ratings: unchanged at 20 Buys, 8 Holds, no Sells (from 20, 6 and 1 at 30-Sep and 21, 5 and 1 at 31-Aug). The Buy share has fallen from 78% to 71% while the SP rose; as in September 2024 to June 2025, Carlsberg's ratings have moved against the SP while its PTs have moved with it.

Net Upgrade Breadth is positive on every measure on the 3-Nov count, for the first time since March 2026. EPS +15% (11 raising, 7 cutting, of 26), EBITDA +55% (13 raising, 1 cutting, of 22), revenue +56% (15 raising, 1 cutting, of 25), PT +28% (11 raising, 4 cutting, of 25). At 30-Sep they were (32%), (29%), (17%) and (22%). Where they diverge: EPS breadth is the weakest, with 7 analysts still cutting EPS while only 1 cuts revenue or EBITDA: plausibly cuts below the operating line rather than in the business, though the sources read do not say.

On 2-Nov you wrote: "SS Upgrade Breadth positive for all metrics". Confirmed on the 3-Nov count above. The raises are mostly currency (item 4 (Consensus by named FY)), so the breadth is wide but shallow.

Where analysts are cutting: Rothschild & Co Redburn cut its FY2027 organic revenue growth by 50 basis points to 3.0% (29-Oct) on "persistent consumer weakness in China that is likely to continue through the first half of 2027", and projects China's operating margin down 180 basis points to 22% in 2026 (AlphaSense, 29-Oct). Bernstein trimmed FY2026 EPS 0.1% (30-Oct). The other EPS cutters are not named in the sources read.

9. Peers

Carlsberg and Heineken are the cohort's leaders at 52W highs; since the 29-Oct statement Carlsberg has outrun every brewer, and it now leads its cohort on revenue breadth as well as on price, a reversal of a week ago.

  • SP technicals: Carlsberg closed 0.5% and Heineken 0.8% below the 52W highs both set on 2-Nov; AB InBev closed 3.7% below its 22-Oct high; Royal Unibrew 27.4% below its 23-Feb high and still 4.2% below its absolute 200D MA. From the 29-Oct close to 3-Nov Carlsberg rose 7.1%, Royal Unibrew 5.6%, Heineken 3.9%, Diageo 3.6%, AB InBev 3.3% and Coca-Cola HBC 2.9%.
  • 3-Nov: every large brewer fell with Consumer staples; Carlsberg's (0.5%) against Heineken (0.8%), AB InBev (1.4%) and Diageo (2.4%), with the volumes in item 2 (Volume, and relative price against its 52W high). Only Royal Unibrew rose (+0.6%, on 0.57x).
  • Sell-side estimates: over three months Carlsberg's EPS is flat (0%) and its revenue +1%; AB InBev's EPS (1%), Heineken's 0%, Royal Unibrew's (2%). Revenue Net Upgrade Breadth (the peer table's basis): Carlsberg +52%, Heineken +15%, Royal Unibrew +7%, AB InBev (20%). A week ago Carlsberg's was (8%), behind Heineken and Royal Unibrew.

Peers' newsflow and guidance (AlphaSense weekly report of 4-Nov):

  • Heineken beat on volume in its third quarter, organic volume +3.9% against 1.8% consensus and Asia Pacific +15.1%, with Vietnam, India and Ethiopia growing in the high teens to low twenties; FY2026 organic operating profit guidance of 2% to 6% maintained (Bernstein, 29-Oct). Bernstein kept Outperform (PT €126.00); Rothschild & Co Redburn noted on 30-Oct that Heineken is "accelerating its operational execution under new leadership".
  • Coca-Cola Europacific Partners beat on its third quarter on 3-Nov: sales 1.0% above consensus, currency-neutral sales +5.8% against 5.0%, volume +3.6%, guidance reiterated (Deutsche Bank, 3-Nov). Bernstein's Underperform ("priced to perfection") and Morgan Stanley's caution on 2027 comparisons are dated 4-Nov, after the 3-Nov close, and are not in the SP readings. For Carlsberg's soft drinks, the read-across is positive for 2026 and a caution for 2027.
  • The 29-Oct results day split the brewers along China exposure (Sector Digest, 30-Oct): AB InBev (3.2%), Carlsberg (3.0%) and Campari (6.6%) fell on guidance kept but not raised; Heineken, growing in China, held. AB InBev's China volume fell 17% against Carlsberg's 10% (Rothschild & Co Redburn; earnings review).

What the peers imply for Carlsberg. The group's move still sets Beverages' Sector RS, which fell 7 points on one down day for Diageo and AB InBev, so a sell signal from Sector RS below 50 with Stock RS below 70 would come from the group more than from Carlsberg. Carlsberg's edge since 29-Oct is its own (Stock RS rising faster than the sector's, revenue breadth now the cohort's best), but its daily moves still track Heineken and Royal Unibrew most closely (average correlation 0.71 each). What would tell: Heineken confirming its own breakout supports Carlsberg's; AB InBev failing again near its 22-Oct high (€75.82) on heavy volume would be the cohort's first sign of distribution at the top.

What the rules say for Carlsberg now, and what decides the next step

  • The finding that matters most: the breakout held its first session on light volume, but it rests on one heavy reversal day, not on heavy buying at the high. Up/down volume over the advance from 18-Sep is 1.06, thinner than Carlsberg's two successful advances of the past year, and relative price is still 6.4% below its February high.
  • Second: the sell-side has turned, wide but shallow. Net Upgrade Breadth is positive on all four measures and the average PT rose 2.0% to DKK 1,065 (from DKK 1,044 at the 28-Oct reading), but FY2027 EPS is up 0.7% over the month on currency and still down over three and six months, so your upgrading rule of 3-Nov is not yet met on the record.
  • Third: on Carlsberg's own record, the rising-MA retest you are waiting for has come late and deep. After the February and July highs no retest of the 50D, 100D or 150D MA held; both lows came 2.7% to 3.3% below the absolute 200D MA, 27 and 36 sessions after the high.
  • The rules today: no sell signal from any of Carlsberg's six Sell Criteria; no tranche is due for sale; all three tranches are held.
  • The nearest sell test, Sector RS below 50 with Stock RS below 70 (read at the Mon 30-Nov month-end): Carlsberg's Stock RS is 76, 7 points above a sell-signal reading; it moved +3 points since the Fri 23-Oct reading, +28 since Fri 2-Oct and +8 since the 31-Jul month-end. Beverages' Sector RS is 74, 25 points above; it moved (11), +24 and +6 over the same spans. About 3% of underperformance of the Stoxx Europe 600 takes Carlsberg's Stock RS below 70 (about 2.5 points per 1% of relative price, 29-Oct to 2-Nov); the Sector RS half needs Beverages to give back its October gain, as it fell 28 points in three sessions in August (58 on 7-Aug to 30 on 12-Aug).
  • How the tests interact: a group-wide fall could meet both halves at once; a Carlsberg-only fall meets only the Stock RS half. Relative price more than 25% below its 52W high needs Carlsberg's SP at DKK 802.0 if the Stoxx Europe 600 is flat (19.8% below the 3-Nov close); a tranche's 30% fall below its highest close since purchase needs the SP at DKK 703.85 (29.7% below the 3-Nov close). A whole-holding sell signal sells Carlsberg when its replacement is bought, under your one-off in/out link.
  • The record: Sector RS below 50 with Stock RS below 70 gave its sell signals after Carlsberg's last two tops late, near the lows: at the 31-Mar month-end at DKK 803.2 (18.2% below the 12-Feb high) and at the 31-Aug month-end at DKK 883.4 (8.4% below the 30-Jul high).
  • The next reading day, Fri 6-Nov: Industry RS below 30 (Consumer staples 66) and the relative 200D MA flat or falling over the month (+0.57%); neither can plausibly give a sell signal.
  • Adds: the rules allow an add: no sell signal, and 2.16% of room below the 6.68% limit on Carlsberg's current 4.52% of capital (3.98% at entry), which takes one 1.67% tranche.
  • Where Carlsberg stands for an add: none of the three stages today. One 0.5% down session at a 52W high is not yet pulling back or basing, not a retest of a rising MA and not a volatility contraction pattern. The Master Dashboard's tests point to the first stage, pulling back or basing (basing plausible, 3 of 4; pulling back possible, 2 of 4; healthy retest possible, 3 of 6, no MA retested; volatility contraction pattern 0 of 4); my reading is that Carlsberg enters that stage only with a pullback larger than its usual weekly move (3.3%).
  • Your add triggers: a positive retest of a rising MA is absent; a broadening of the case is partly present (breadth, PTs, technicals and RS up; guidance reiterated, not raised; estimates up only on currency).
  • For your ruling: on 2-Nov you wrote "SUMMARY - Add 2-4% in 2 tranches over N2W". Carlsberg's 2.16% of room takes one 1.67% tranche; a second would be limited to 0.49% by the 6.68% limit, so your two tranches (2 x 1.67% = 3.34%) do not fit.

What the rules say for Hexatronic now, and what decides the next step

  • The pullback paused: Hexatronic rose 1.4% on 3-Nov on light volume, after an intraday lower low, while its index, sector, industry and peers fell; in the June and July tops the third session after the high fell again (items 1 and 2).

  • Hexatronic is the strong outlier in a weakening group: Hardware's Sector RS is 18 and Technology's Industry RS 73 and falling, while Hexatronic's Stock RS is 96 (items 3 and 9).
  • Each tranche today: hold. Hexatronic is a Middle Innings Core Investment governed by the six Sell Criteria alone; none gives a sell signal, and all four tranches (5.49% of capital at current value; 4.95% at entry) hold.
  • The nearest sell test, relative price more than 25% below its 52W high, read at the 30-Nov month-end: 11.6% below on 3-Nov, from 11.7% a week ago (27-Oct), 14.5% four weeks ago (6-Oct) and 19.6% thirteen weeks ago (6-Aug).
